The number
\mathbb{O}(A)
is called T-tracial approximate oscillation of
A.

Suppose that
S\subset\widetilde{QT}(A)\setminus\{0\}
is a compact convex set such that

\mathbb{R}_{+}\cdot S=\widetilde{QT}(A).

By (the “Moreover” part of) Proposition
4.9
, if
\mathbb{O}(A)=0,

then
A
has T-tracial approximate oscillation zero.
Conversely,
if
A
has
T
-tracial approximate oscillation zero,
then
\mathbb{O}(A)=0
(see (4) of Proposition
2.10
).

The next example shows that there are (commutative)
C^{*}
-algebras
A
of stable rank one such that
\mathbb{O}(A)>0.

By Theorem
1.1
, if
A
is a separable simple
C^{*}
-algebra  which has strict comparison but does not have stable rank one,
then
\mathbb{O}(A)>0.

However, we
do not have any such examples.

Example 5.3
.

Let
A=C([0,1]).
Then
T(A)
is compact and
{\widetilde{QT}}(A)=\widetilde{T}(A).
Let

{{a\in}}A_{+}\setminus\{0\}
be such that

0\leq a\leq 1
that is not invertible. Let
G=\{t\in[0,1]:a(t)>0\}.
Then
G
is an open subset.
Note
0\in{\rm sp}(a).

Since
A
has no non-trivial projection, there are
t_{n}\in{\rm sp}(a)

with
\lim_{n\to\infty}t_{n}=0.
For any
0<\delta<1/2,
let
b=f_{\delta}(a).

Let
s_{n}\in G
such that
a(s_{n})=t_{n},

n\in\mathbb{N}.

Then, for some
0<\eta<\delta/2,
there is
s_{n}
in the support of
c=f_{\eta}(a)-f_{\delta/2}(a).

There is a Borel probability measure
\mu_{n}
on
[0,1]
such
that
\mu({{\{s_{n}\}}})=1.
Let
\tau_{\mu_{n}}
be the tracial state induced by
\mu_{n},

then
\tau_{\mu_{n}}(f_{\eta/2}(a)-b)=1.

This implies that
\omega(a)|_{S_{a}}=1.

This also holds for any non-zero
g\in\mathrm{Her}(a)_{+}\subset A_{+}.

In other words, for any
g\in\mathrm{Her}(a)_{+},

\omega(g)|_{S_{a}}=1.
Therefore

\Omega^{T}(a)=1.
It follows
\mathbb{O}(A)=1.

Recall that
A
has stable rank one.

Proposition 5.4
.

If
A
is a simple
C^{*}
-algebra  which has (T-) tracial approximate
oscillation zero, then every hereditary
C^{*}
-subalgebra  of
A
also has (T-) tracial approximate
oscillation zero.

Proof.

This follows from the definition immediately.
∎

Definition 5.5
.

Let
S
be a compact subset of
{{{\widetilde{QT}}}}(A)\setminus\{0\}

such that
\widetilde{QT}(A)={{\mathbb{R}_{+}}}\cdot S
and

B\subset A
be a hereditary
C^{*}
-subalgebra.
A sequence of elements
\{e_{n}\}\subset{\rm Ped}(B)_{+}^{\bf 1}

is said to be tracial approximate identity for

B,

if, for any
b\in B,

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{cu}(\iota(b)-\iota(b)\{e_{n}\})\|={{0,}}

(e 5.2)

and
\{e_{n}\}
is said to be
T
-tracial approximate identity for
B
(relative to
S
), if, for any
b\in B

with
\|b\|_{{}_{2,S}}<\infty,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|b-be_{n}\|_{{}_{2,S}}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\|b-e_{n}b\|_{{}_{2,S}}=0.

(e 5.3)

We do not require that
\{e_{n}\}
is increasing.

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
A
be a
C^{*}
-algebra,
a\in(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}
with
\|a\|_{{}_{2,S}}<\infty
and
S\subset{\widetilde{QT}}(A)\setminus\{0\}
be a compact subset.

(1) Then
\Omega^{N}(a)|_{S}=0
if and only

{\rm Her}(a)
has a (not necessarily increasing) approximate identity
\{e_{n}\}
such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega_{n}(e_{n})|{{{}_{S}}}=0.

(2) Moreover,
\Omega^{T}(a)|_{S}=0
(or
\Omega^{T}_{C}(a)|_{S}=0
) if and only if

{\rm Her}(a)
admits a
T
-tracial (or tracial) approximate identity (relative to
S
)
\{e_{n}\}
with

\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n})|_{S}=0.

Proof.

For (1), let us assume
that
\{e_{n}\}
is a (not necessarily increasing) approximate identity
for
\mathrm{Her}(a)
such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n})=0.

Then

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|a-e_{n}^{1/2}ae_{n}^{1/2}\|=0.

(e 5.4)

By Lemma
4.10
,

\omega(e_{n}^{1/2}ae_{n}^{1/2})|_{S}\leq\omega(e_{n})|_{S}\to 0.

Thus
\Omega^{N}(a)|_{S}=0.

Conversely,
suppose that
a\in(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}^{\bf 1}

and
\{b_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}({\rm Her}(a))_{+}

such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|a-b_{n}\|=0\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(b_{n})|_{S}=0.

(e 5.5)

Note that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|b_{n}\|=\|a\|\leq 1.

Let
g\in C([0,\infty))_{+}^{\bf 1}
such that

g(t)=t
if
t\in[0,1]
and
g(t)=1
if
t>1.

Then, for any
n\in\mathbb{N},

g(b_{n})\sim b_{n}.

We also have
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|g(a)-g(b_{n})\|=0.
But
g(a)=a.

Then, replacing
b_{n}
by
g(b_{n}),
we may assume that
that
\|b_{n}\|\leq 1.

For each
k\in\mathbb{N},
choose
{{n_{k}}}
such that

\displaystyle\|b_{n_{k}}^{1/k}-a^{1/k}\|<1/k,\,\,k\in\mathbb{N}.

(e 5.6)

Put
e_{k}=b_{n_{k}}^{1/k},\,k\in\mathbb{N}.

Then, for any
x\in{\rm Her}(a),

\displaystyle\|x-xe_{k}\|\leq\|x-xa^{1/k}\|+\|x\|\|a^{1/k}-e_{k}\|\leq\|x-xa^{1/k}\|+{\|x\|\over{k}}.

(e 5.7)

This shows that
\{e_{k}\}
is a (not necessarily increasing) approximate identity for
{\rm Her}(a).

Since
e_{k}=b_{n_{k}}^{1/k},
we have that
\omega(e_{k})=\omega(b_{n_{k}})
and

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\omega(e_{k})|_{S}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\omega(b_{n_{k}})|_{S}=0.

For (2), let us prove one case.

Fix
a\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}.
Without loss of generality, we may assume
that
0\leq a\leq 1.

Suppose that
\{e_{n}\}
is a T-tracial approximate identity of
{\rm Her}(a)
relative to
S.

Then

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|a-e_{n}a\|_{{}_{2,S}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|a-ae_{n}\|_{{}_{2,S}}=0\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|a-e_{n}ae_{n}\|^{2/3}_{{}_{2,S}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}(\|a-e_{n}a\|^{2/3}_{{}_{2,S}}+\|e_{n}a-e_{n}ae_{n}\|^{2/3}_{{}_{2,S}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|e_{n}(a-ae_{n})\|_{{}_{2,S}}^{2/3}\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}(\|e_{n}^{2}\|^{{{1/3}}}\|a-ae_{n}\|_{{}_{2,S}}^{2/3})=0.

By Lemma
4.10
,
\omega(e_{n}ae_{n})|_{S}\leq\omega(e_{n}^{2})|_{S}=\omega(e_{n})|_{S}\to 0.

Thus
\Omega^{T}(a)|_{S}=0.

Conversely, suppose that
\Omega^{T}(a)|_{S}=0.
Then, by Proposition
4.8
,
there exists
\{b_{n}\}\subset{{{\rm Ped}({\rm Her}(a))}}_{+}^{\bf 1}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|a-b_{n}\|_{{}_{2,S}}=0\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(b_{n})|_{S}=0.

(e 5.8)

Let
B={\rm Her}(a)

and
\Pi_{S}:l^{\infty}(B)\to l^{\infty}(B)/I_{{}_{S,B}}

be the quotient map (see Definition
4.7
for
I_{{}_{S,B}}
).
Then

\displaystyle\Pi_{S}(\iota(a))=\Pi_{S}(\{b_{n}\}).

(e 5.9)

For each
k\in\mathbb{N},
we have

\displaystyle\Pi_{S}({{\iota(a)^{1/k}}})=\Pi_{S}(\{b_{n}\}^{1/k}).

(e 5.10)

It follows that, for each
k\in\mathbb{N},
there exists
n(k)\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\|a^{1/k}-b_{n(k)}^{1/k}\|_{{}_{2,S}}<1/2k.

(e 5.11)

Choose
e_{k}=b_{n(k)}^{1/k},

k\in\mathbb{N}.

Then, for any
c\in{\rm Her}(a),

\displaystyle\|c-ce_{k}\|^{2/3}_{{{}_{2,S}}}\leq\|c-ca^{1/k}\|^{2/3}_{{}_{2,S}}+\|c(a^{1/k}-b_{n(k)}^{1/k})\|^{2/3}_{{}_{2,S}}\to 0,\,\,\,{\rm as}\,\,k\to\infty.

(e 5.12)

Since
b_{n(k)}\sim e_{k},

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\omega(e_{k})|_{S}=0.

(e 5.13)

The proposition then follows.
∎

Proposition 5.7
.

Let
A
be a
\sigma
-unital
C^{*}
-algebra,

S\subset\widetilde{QT}(A)
be a compact subset,
and let
a\in{{{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}.}}

Then
\Omega^{T}(a)|_{S}=0

if and only if
\Omega^{N}(a)|_{S}=0.

Proof.

For the “if” part, let us assume

\Omega^{N}(a)|_{S}=0.

By the definition there is

\{b_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}(\mathrm{Her}(a))_{+}

such that
\omega(b_{n})|_{S}<1/n

and
\|a-b_{n}\|<1/n.

Let
b_{n}^{\prime}=\frac{\|a\|b_{n}}{\|b_{n}\|+1/n}.

Then
\|b_{n}^{\prime}\|\leq\|a\|,

\displaystyle{{\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(b_{n}^{\prime})|_{S}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(b_{n})|_{S}=0,\lim_{n\to\infty}\|b_{n}-b_{n}^{\prime}\|=0\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,}}

(e 5.14)

\displaystyle 0\leq\Omega^{T}(a)|_{S}\leq\|\Pi_{S}(\iota(a)-\{b_{n}^{\prime}\})\|\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\|a-b_{n}^{\prime}\|{{\leq}}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\|a-b_{n}\|=0.

(e 5.15)

For the “only if” part, let us
assume that
\Omega^{T}(a)|_{S}=0.

Then, by Proposition
5.6
, there are
e_{n}\in{{\mathrm{Her}(a)}}_{+}^{\bf 1}

such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n})|_{S}=0\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,\lim_{n\to\infty}\|a-a^{1/2}e_{n}a^{1/2}\|_{{}_{2,S}}=0.

(e 5.16)

It follows that
\{b_{n}\}=\{a-a^{1/2}e_{n}a^{1/2}\}\in{{(I_{{}_{S}})_{+}}}
(see
2.16
for
the definition of
I_{{}_{S}}
).
By (4) of
Proposition
4.8
, there exists
\{c_{n}\}\in(I_{{}_{S}})_{{+}}
such
that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup\{d_{\tau}(c_{n}):\tau\in S\}=0\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,\lim_{n\to\infty}\|b_{n}-c_{n}\|=0.

(e 5.17)

Put
d_{n}=a^{1/2}e_{n}a^{1/2}+c_{n},

n\in\mathbb{N}.
Then
d_{n}\geq 0.

Put
{\bar{d_{n}}}={\|a\|d_{n}\over{\|d_{n}\|+1/n}},

n\in\mathbb{N}.
Then
\|{\bar{d}}_{n}\|\leq\|a\|
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}.

Since
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|a-d_{n}\|=0,
we have
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|d_{n}\|=\|a\|.

It follows that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|a-{\bar{d}}_{n}\|=0.

(e 5.18)

On the other hand, by Proposition
4.4
(1) (see also (2) of Proposition
2.10
)
for all
\tau\in S

\displaystyle\omega(d_{n})(\tau)|_{S}\leq\omega(a^{1/2}e_{n}a^{1/2})(\tau)|_{S}+\overline{d_{\tau}}(c_{n})|_{S}\leq\omega(a^{1/2}e_{n}a^{1/2})|_{S}+\sup\{d_{\tau}(c_{n}):\tau\in S\}

(e 5.19)

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}.

By Lemma
4.10
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(a^{1/2}e_{n}a^{1/2})=\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n})=0.

By
the fact that
\bar{d}_{n}\sim d_{n}
and
Proposition
4.2
,
we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega({\bar{d}}_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(d_{n})\overset{\eqref{F109-2}}{=}\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\tau\in S}\{\omega(d_{n})(\tau)|_{S}\}

(e 5.20)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{F111-3}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(a^{1/2}e_{n}a^{1/2})|_{S}+\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup\{d_{\tau}(c_{n}):\tau\in S\}=0.

Then (
e 5.18
) and (
e 5.20
)
show that
\Omega^{N}(a)|_{S}=0.

∎

Let us present some examples of
C^{*}
-algebras which have norm approximate oscillation zero.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
A
be a
C^{*}
-algebra  of real rank zero.
Then
A
has norm approximate oscillation zero.

Proof.

Let
a\in{\rm Ped}(A)
with
0\leq a\leq 1.

Put
B={\rm Her}(a).
Then
B
has an approximate identity
\{e_{n}\}
consisting of projections.
Note that, since
e_{n}
is a projection,
\widehat{[e_{n}]}=\widehat{e_{n}}
is continuous on
S.

The proposition follows from Proposition
5.6

∎

Let
T_{b}=\{s\in{\widetilde{QT}}(A):s(b)=1\}
for some nonzero

b\in({\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K}))_{+}.
It is a compact convex subset and
T_{b}
is a basis
for the cone
{{\widetilde{QT}}}(A)
if
A
is simple. The proof of the following
is taken from Lemma 4.8 of
[
22
]
(see also Remark 4.7 of
[
22
]
).

Theorem 5.9
.

Let
A
be a
C^{*}
-algebra  with countable
\partial_{e}(T_{b})
(for some
b\in{\rm Ped}(A)_{+}\setminus\{0\}
),
where
\partial_{e}(T_{b})
is the set of extremal points of
T_{b}.
Then

\displaystyle\Omega^{N}(a)=0\,\,\,\text{for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,a\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}.

(e 5.21)

In particular,
A
has norm approximate oscillation zero.

Proof.

We may assume that
a\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}\setminus\{0\}
and

0\leq a\leq 1.

If
0\in\overline{{{\mathbb{R}_{+}}}\setminus{\rm sp}(a)},
then
\Omega^{N}(a)=0.

To see this, let
s_{n}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\setminus{\rm sp}(a)
such that

s_{n}\searrow 0.

Then the characteristic functions
\chi_{[s_{n},1]}
is continuous on
{\rm sp}(a).

Therefore
p_{n}=\chi_{[s_{n},1]}(a)\in C^{*}(a)\subset{\rm Her}(a)
is a projection. Note
that
p_{n}\leq p_{n+1}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}.

Then

\displaystyle\|a-p_{n}a\|{{\leq}}s_{n}\to 0.

(e 5.22)

In other words
\{p_{n}\}
is an approximate identity for
\mathrm{Her}(a).

Moreover
\omega(p_{n})=0.
So this case follows from
Proposition
5.6
.

Therefore, without loss of generality, we may assume that
[0,r]\subset{\rm sp}(a)
for some

0<r<1.

Let
r/2>\eta>0.

Note that, since
a\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+},

\sup\{d_{\tau}(a):\tau\in T_{b}\}=M<\infty
(see
2.10
).
For each
\tau\in\partial_{e}(T_{b}),

\tau
induces a Borel measure
\mu_{\tau}
on
{\rm sp}(a)
which is bounded by
M.

We claim that there is
s\in(r-\eta,r]
such that

\displaystyle\sup\{\mu_{\tau}(\{s\}):\tau\in T_{b}\}=0.

(e 5.23)

To see this, write
\partial_{e}(T_{b})=\{\tau_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{\prime}},
where

\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
is a subset of
\mathbb{N}.
For each
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\prime},
and
n\in\mathbb{N},
define

\displaystyle S_{k,n}=\{s\in(r-\eta,r]:\mu_{\tau_{k}}(\{s\})\geq 1/n\}.

(e 5.24)

Since
\mu_{\tau_{k}}({\rm sp}(a))\leq M,

S_{k,n}
must be finite.
It follows that

S_{k}=\cup_{n=1}^{\infty}S_{k,n}
is countable.
Thus
S=\cup_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{\prime}}S_{k}
is countable.
Therefore there must be
s\in(r-\eta,r]
such that
s\not\in S.
In other words, there must be

s\in(r-\eta,r]
such that

\displaystyle\mu_{\tau}(\{s\})=0\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\partial_{e}(T_{b}).

(e 5.25)

Since
T_{b}
is compact, by the Krein-Milman Theorem, this implies that

\displaystyle\mu_{\tau}(\{s\})=0\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in T_{b}.

(e 5.26)

This proves the claim.

By the claim, for each integer
n\in\mathbb{N},

there is
\delta>0
such that
1/2^{n+1}<\delta<1/2^{n}

and

\displaystyle\mu_{\tau}(\{\delta\})=0\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in T_{b}.

(e 5.27)

Note that

\displaystyle d_{\tau}((a-\delta)_{+})=\mu_{\tau}((\delta,1]\cap{\rm sp}(a))\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in T_{b}

(e 5.28)

is a lower semi-continuous function.
By Portmanteau Theorem, the function
h:T_{b}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
given by

\displaystyle h(\tau):=\mu_{\tau}([\delta,1]\cap{\rm sp}(a))

(e 5.29)

is an upper semi-continuous function.
1
1

1

This can be directly obtained as follows:
Let
f^{(n)}\in C_{0}((0,\|a\|{{]}})_{+}^{\bf 1}
be such that

f^{(n)}(t)=1
for
t\in[{{\delta}},\|a\|],

f^{(n)}=0

if
t\in[0,{{\delta}}-\delta/2^{n}]
and linear in
[{{\delta}}-\delta/2^{n},{{\delta}}].

Then
\tau(f^{(n)}(a))\searrow h(\tau).

By (
e 5.27
),

\displaystyle h(\tau)=d_{\tau}({{(a-\delta)_{+}}})\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in T_{b}.

(e 5.30)

It follows that
d_{\tau}({{(a-\delta)_{+}}})
is continuous.

To prove the lemma, let
\varepsilon>0.
Choose
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
1/2^{n}<\varepsilon.

Choose
1/2^{n+1}<\delta_{n}<1/2^{n}
such that
\mu_{\tau}(\{\delta_{n}\})=0

for all
\tau\in S
as above.
Put
d_{n}={{(a-\delta_{n})_{+}.}}

Then

\displaystyle\|a-d_{n}\|<\varepsilon.

(e 5.31)

Since
d_{\tau}(d_{n})=d_{\tau}({{(a-\delta_{n})_{+}}}),
we have that

\omega({{d_{n}}})(\tau)|_{T_{b}}=0
for all
\tau\in T_{b}.

The lemma follows.
∎

The following is a restatement of Lemma 7.2 of
[
10
]

with the same proof (and with some necessary modification and correcting a typo).

Theorem 5.10
.

Let
A
be a
\sigma
-unital simple
C^{*}
-algebra
which has strict comparison and almost stable rank one.
Suppose that the canonical map
\Gamma:{\rm Cu}(A)\to{\rm LAff}_{+}({\widetilde{QT}}(A))
is surjective
(see
2.13
).
Then
A
has norm approximate oscillation zero.

Proof.

Let
e_{A}\in{\rm Ped}(A)_{+}\setminus\{0\}
and
A_{1}=\mathrm{Her}(e_{A}).

Then
{\rm Ped}(A_{1})=A_{1}.
Since
A
is
\sigma
-unital,
A\otimes{\cal K}\cong A_{1}\otimes{\cal K}
by Brown’s stable isomorphism theorem (
[
4
]
).
Therefore it suffices to show that
A_{1}
has norm approximate oscillation zero.
To simplify notation, we may assume that
A=A_{1}
(and
A={\rm Ped}(A)
).

Let
a\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}^{\bf 1}.

It follows from the proof of Lemma 7.2 of
[
10
]
that, since
\mathrm{Her}(a)
also has strict comparison,
almost stable rank one and
\Gamma
is surjective,

\mathrm{Her}(a)
has an approximate identity consisting of elements
\{e_{n}\}
such
that
\widehat{e_{n}}
is continuous on
\overline{QT(A)}^{w}.
Then the theorem follows from Proposition

5.6
.
Since we do not assume that
QT(A)=T(A),
to be complete,
let us repeat some of the argument in the proof of Lemma 7.2 of
[
10
]

which will be, again, used in the proof of
8.9
.

By Proposition
5.6
,
it suffices to show that, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2),

there is
e\in\mathrm{Her}(a)_{+}^{\bf 1}

such that
f_{\varepsilon}(a)\leq e
and
\widehat{[e]}
is continuous.
By the first part of the proof of Theorem
5.9
, we may assume that

[0,\varepsilon_{0})\subset{\rm sp}(a)
for some
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,\varepsilon).

Note that, without loss of generality, we may assume that

\displaystyle d_{\tau}(f_{{{\varepsilon/2}}}(a))<\tau(f_{\delta_{1}}(a))<d_{\tau}(f_{\eta_{1}}(a))<\tau(f_{\delta_{2}}(a))<d_{\tau}(f_{\eta}(a))\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w},

(e 5.32)

where
\varepsilon/4>\delta_{1},\delta_{1}/2>\eta_{1},\eta_{1}/2>\delta_{2},
and
\delta_{2}/2>\eta>0.

Put
h_{i}(\tau)=\tau(f_{\delta_{i}}(a))\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in{{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}},

i=1,2.

Then
h_{i}\in\operatorname{Aff}_{+}(\overline{QT(A)}^{w}).

Since
\Gamma
is surjective, there is
c\in(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}^{{{\bf 1}}}
such
that
d_{\tau}(c)=h_{2}(\tau)
for all
\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}.

Since
A
has strict comparison,
(
e 5.32
) and the choice of
c

implies
c\lesssim f_{\eta}(a).
Since
A
has almost stable rank one, by Lemma 3.2 of
[
10
]

and
(
e 5.32
),
there is
x\in A\otimes{\cal K}
such
that

\displaystyle x^{*}x=c\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,xx^{*}\in{\rm Her}(f_{\eta}(a)).

(e 5.33)

Put
c_{0}=xx^{*}.
Then
0\leq c_{0}\leq 1.
Note that

d_{\tau}(c_{0})=d_{\tau}(c)
for all
\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}.

Since
h_{1}
is
continuous,

h_{1}(\tau)<h_{2}(\tau)=d_{\tau}(c)=d_{\tau}(c_{0})=\lim_{n\to\infty}\tau(f_{1/n}(c_{0}))

for all
\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w},

and
\overline{QT(A)}^{w}
is compact,
there is an integer
m>2
such that

\displaystyle h_{1}(\tau)<\tau(f_{1/m}(c_{0}))\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}.

(e 5.34)

By (
e 5.32
) and Lemma 3.2 of
[
10
]
, there is a unitary

u
in the unitization of
\mathrm{Her}(f_{\eta}(a))
such that

\displaystyle u^{*}f_{\varepsilon/8}(f_{{{\varepsilon/2}}}(a))u\in\mathrm{Her}(f_{1/m}(c_{0})).

(e 5.35)

Set
c_{1}=uc_{0}u^{*}.
Then

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon/8}(f_{{\varepsilon/2}}(a))\in\mathrm{Her}(f_{1/m}(c_{1}))\subset\mathrm{Her}(c_{1}).

(e 5.36)

There is
g\in C_{0}((0,1])
such that
0\leq g\leq 1,

g(t)>0
for all
t\in(0,1],
and

\displaystyle gf_{1/2m}=f_{1/2m}.

(e 5.37)

Put
e=g(c_{1}).
Then
[e]=[c_{1}]=[c_{0}]=[c].

In particular,
d_{\tau}(e)
is continuous on
\overline{QT(A)}^{w}.

But we also have, by (
e 5.36
),

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}(a)\leq f_{\varepsilon/8}(f_{{\varepsilon/2}}(a))\leq e.

(e 5.38)

This completes the proof.

∎

6
C^{*}
-algebra
l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}

Definition 6.1
.

Let
A
be a compact
C^{*}
-algebra  and
let
p\in l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}
(or in
l^{\infty}(A)/N_{cu}(A)
) be a projection and

\{e_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}(A)_{+}^{\bf 1}
such that
\Pi(\{e_{n}\})=p

(recall that
\Pi:l^{\infty}(A)\to l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}
is the quotient map).
The sequence
\{e_{n}\}
is said to be a permanent projection lifting,
if for any sequence of positive integers
\{m(n)\},

\displaystyle\Pi(\{e_{n}^{1/m(n)}\})=p\,\,\,(or\,\,\Pi_{cu}(\{e_{n}^{1/m(n)}\})=p).

(e 6.1)

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
A
be a compact
C^{*}
-algebra
with
{\widetilde{QT}}(A)\setminus\{0\}\not=\emptyset
and
\{e_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}(A)_{+}^{\bf 1}.

(1) Let
p=\Pi(\{e_{n}\})
(or
p=\Pi_{cu}(\{e_{n}\})
) be a projection.
Then
\{f_{\delta}(e_{n})\}
is a permanent projection lifting of
p
for any
0<\delta<1/2
(for both cases)
and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup\{\tau(e_{n}-f_{\delta}(e_{n})e_{n}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}=0\,\,\,({\rm or}\,\,{{\{}}e_{n}-e_{n}^{1/2}f_{\delta}(e_{n})e_{n}^{1/2}{{\}}}\in N_{cu}).

(e 6.2)

(2) If
\{e_{n}\}
is a permanent projection lifting, then
\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n})=0.
Moreover,
an element
\{e_{n}\}
is a permanent projection lifting (from
\l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}
) if and only if

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup\{d_{\tau}(e_{n})-\tau(e_{n}^{2}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}=0.

(3) If
\{e_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}(A)_{+}^{\bf 1}
and
\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n})=0

then for some
l(k)\in\mathbb{N},

p=\Pi(\{e_{k}^{{{1/l(k)}}}\})

is a projection,
and
\{e_{k}^{{{1/{l(k)}}}}\}
is a permanent projection lifting of
p.

(4) Suppose that
{{p=}}\Pi_{{{cu}}}(\{e_{n}\})
is a projection
for some
\{e_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}(A)_{+}^{\bf 1}.
Then
\{e_{n}\}
is a permanent projection lifting (from
\l^{\infty}(A)/N_{cu}
) if

g_{\delta}(e_{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle c.}}{{\to}}0
for some
\delta\in(0,1/4).

(5) If
\{e_{n}\}
is a permanent projection lifting of
p\in l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}},

then

l^{\infty}(\{\mathrm{Her}(e_{n})\})/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}=p(l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}})p

(see
2.15
for

l^{\infty}(\{\mathrm{Her}(e_{n})\})
).

(6) If
A
is algebraically simple and
QT(A)\not=\emptyset
and
e\in A_{+}^{\bf 1}
is a strictly positive element such that
\widehat{{{[e]}}}
is continuous
on
\overline{QT(A)}^{w},
then
l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}
is unital.

(7) A
\sigma
-unital simple
C^{*}
-algebra
A

has continuous scale if and only if

l^{\infty}(A)/N_{cu}
is unital.

Proof.

(1) Note that
\Pi(f_{\delta}(\{e_{n}\}))=f_{\delta}(\Pi(\{e_{n}\}))=p
for any
0<\delta<1/2.

Therefore
\Pi(f_{\delta/2}(\{e_{n}\}))=p.
Put
b_{n}=f_{\delta}(e_{n}),

n\in\mathbb{N}.

For any integers
\{m(n)\},
we have

\displaystyle b_{n}^{1/m(n)}\leq f_{\delta/2}(e_{n}),\,\,n\in\mathbb{N}.

(e 6.3)

It follows that

\displaystyle p=\Pi(\{f_{\delta}(e_{n})\})\leq\Pi(\{b_{n}^{1/m(n)}\})\leq\Pi(\{f_{\delta/2}(e_{n})\})=p.

(e 6.4)

This proves the first part of (1)
(the proof for
p=\Pi_{cu}(\{f_{\delta}(e_{n})\})
is similar).

Since

\Pi(\{f_{\delta}(e_{n})\})=p
(or
\Pi_{cu}(\{f_{\delta}(e_{n})\})=p
), we have

\displaystyle e_{n}-f_{\delta}(e_{n})e_{n}\in I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}},\,\,\,{\rm hence}\,\,(e_{n}-f_{\delta}(e_{n})e_{n})^{1/2}\in I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}

(e 6.5)

\displaystyle{\rm(or}\,\,e_{n}-f_{\delta}(e_{n})e_{n},\,\,(e_{n}-f_{\delta}(e_{n})e_{n})^{1/2}\in N_{cu}{\rm)}.

(e 6.6)

Part (1) follows.

(2) If

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup\{d_{\tau}(e_{n})-\tau(e_{n}^{2}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}=0,

then, for any

\{m(n)\}{{\ \subset\mathbb{N}}},

\displaystyle\sup\{\tau(e_{n}^{1/m(n)})-\tau(e_{n}^{2}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}\leq\sup\{d_{\tau}(e_{n})-\tau(e_{n}^{2}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}\to 0.

(e 6.7)

It follows that

\{e_{n}^{1/m(n)}-e_{n}^{2}\}\in I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}.

Since
e_{n}^{2}\leq e_{n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N},
this also implies that
\{e_{n}-e_{n}^{2}\}\in I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}.

Hence
\Pi(\{e_{n}\})
is a projection and
\{e_{n}\}
is a permanent projection lifting.

Now suppose that
\{e_{n}\}
is a permanent projection lifting of
p=\Pi(\{e_{n}\}).

Let us show first
that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n})=0.

Otherwise, there exists a subsequence
\{l(k)\}
such
that
\omega(e_{l(k)})>\sigma
for some
\sigma>0.

Fix any
\delta\in(0,1/4).
By Proposition
4.6
, for each of these
l(k),
there are
m(l(k))
such
that

\displaystyle\sup\{\tau(e_{l(k)}^{1/m(l(k))})-\tau(f_{\delta}(e_{l(k)})):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}>\omega(e_{l(k)})-\sigma/4>\sigma/2.

(e 6.8)

Choose a sequence
m(n)
of integers which extends
m(l(k)).

Then

\displaystyle\limsup_{n}\|(e_{n}^{1/m(n)}-f_{\delta}(e_{n}))^{1/2}\|_{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}\geq\sigma/2.

(e 6.9)

Therefore
\Pi(\{e_{n}^{1/m(n)}\})\not=\Pi(f_{\delta}(\{e_{n}\}))=p.
A contradiction.
Hence

\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n})=0.

Therefore there exists a sequence
\{m(n)\}
such that

\displaystyle\sup\{d_{\tau}(e_{n})-\tau(e_{n}^{1/m(n)}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<1/n,\,\,n\in\mathbb{N}.

(e 6.10)

Then, since
\{(e_{n}^{1/m(n)}-e_{n}^{2})^{1/2}\}\in I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}

(for any
\{m(n)\}
), we also have that

\displaystyle\hskip-36.135pt\sup\{d_{\tau}(e_{n})-\tau(e_{n}^{2}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup\{d_{\tau}(e_{n})-\tau(e_{n}^{1/m(n)}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}+\sup\{\tau(e_{n}^{1/m(n)}-e_{n}^{2}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}

\displaystyle<1/n+\|(e_{n}^{1/m(n)}-e_{n}^{2})^{1/2}\|_{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}\,\,\to 0\,\,\,\,{\rm as\,\,{{n}}\to\infty}.

(e 6.11)

(3) In this case, since
\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n})=0,
there are
l({{n}})\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup\{d_{\tau}(e_{n})-\tau(e_{n}^{1/l({{n}})}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}=0.

(e 6.12)

It follows that

\displaystyle\{e_{{n}}^{1/m({{n}})}-e_{{n}}^{1/l({{n}})}\}\in I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}

(e 6.13)

for any integers
m(n)\geq l({{n}}).

Since

\displaystyle\|e_{{n}}^{1/2{{l(n)}}}-(e_{{n}}^{1/2{{l(n)}}})^{2}\|^{2}{{{}_{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}}}\leq\sup\{d_{\tau}(e_{{n}})-\tau(e_{{n}}^{l({{n}})}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}\to{{0,}}

(e 6.14)

as
{{n}}\to\infty,

\Pi(\{e_{n}^{1/2{{l}}(n)}\})=p
is a projection.
By (
e 6.13
),
for any integers
{{m(n)}}\geq l({{n}}),

\displaystyle\Pi(\{e_{{n}}^{1/{{m(n)}}}\})=\Pi(\{e_{{n}}^{1/{{l(n)}}}\}){{=(\Pi(\{e_{n}^{1/2l(n)}\}))^{2}}}=p.

(e 6.15)

It follows that
\{e_{n}^{{{1/l(n)}}}\}
is a permanent projection lifting of
p.

(4)
Suppose that
g_{\delta}(e_{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle c.}}{{\to}}0
for some
0<\delta<1/4.

We have, for any
m(n)\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle e_{n}^{1/m(n)}-f_{\delta/2}(e_{n})e_{n}^{1/m(n)}\lesssim g_{\delta}(e_{n})\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,n\in\mathbb{N}.

(e 6.16)

It follows that
\{e_{n}^{1/m(n)}-f_{\delta/2}(e_{n})e{{{}_{n}^{1/m(n)}}}\}\in N_{cu}.
One then
checks that

\displaystyle{{\hskip-7.22743pt}}p\leq\Pi_{cu}(\{e_{n}^{1/{{m(n)}}}\})=\Pi_{cu}(\{f_{\delta/2}(e_{n})e{{{}_{n}^{1/m(n)}}}\})\leq\Pi_{cu}(\{f_{\delta/2}(e_{n})\}){{=f_{\delta/2}(\Pi_{cu}(\{e_{n}\}))}}=p.

(e 6.17)

Thus (4) follows.

For (5), let
B=l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}.

It is clear that
pBp\subset\Pi(l^{\infty}({{\{}}{\rm Her}(e_{n}){{\}}})).

Suppose that

{{g\in}}\Pi(l^{\infty}({{\{}}{\rm Her}(e_{n}){{\}}}){{)_{+}^{\bf 1}}}.

Then we may write
g=\Pi(\{g_{n}\})
such that
g_{n}\in({\rm Her}(e_{n}))^{\bf 1}_{+},

n\in\mathbb{N}.

For any
\varepsilon>0,
there exists
m(n)\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\|{{e_{n}^{1/m(n)}}}g_{n}e_{n}^{1/m(n)}-g_{n}\|<\varepsilon\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,n\in\mathbb{N}.

(e 6.18)

Thus

\displaystyle\|\Pi(\{e_{n}^{1/m(n)}\})g\Pi(\{e_{n}^{1/m(n)}\})-g\|<\varepsilon.

(e 6.19)

However, since
\{e_{n}\}
is a permanent projection lifting of
p,

\Pi(\{e_{n}^{1/m(n)}\})=p.

Thus

\displaystyle\|{{p}}gp-g\|<\varepsilon.

(e 6.20)

It follows
g\in pBp.
This shows that
C=pBp=\Pi(l^{\infty}({{\{}}{\rm Her}(e_{n}){{\}}})).

(6) In this case,
\omega(e)=0.

Therefore, by (3),
\{e^{{{1/l(n)}}}\}
is a permanent
projection lifting for
p=\Pi(\{e^{{{1/l(n)}}}\})
(for some
l({{n}})\in\mathbb{N}
).

For any
\{x_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}(A),

there is a sequence
\{m(n)\}
of integers such that

\displaystyle\|x_{n}e^{1/m(n)}-x_{n}\|<1/n\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,\|e^{1/m(n)}x_{n}-x_{n}\|<1/n,\,\,n\in\mathbb{N}.

(e 6.21)

Hence
p\{x_{n}\}=\{x_{n}\}p=\{x_{n}\}.
So
p
is the unit of
l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}.

For (7),
suppose that
A
has continuous scale.
Let
e\in A_{+}^{\bf 1}
be a strictly positive element.
By (3) of Proposition
4.4
,
\omega^{c}(e)=0.

Then, for any
\varepsilon>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N},
there exists an integer
l(n)\in\mathbb{N}

such that

\displaystyle{{f_{\varepsilon}(e^{1/(m(n)}-e^{1/l(n)})\lesssim g_{1/n}(e)\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,any}\,\,\,m(n)>l(n).}}

(e 6.22)

Since
g_{1/n}(e)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle c.}}{{\to}}0
(see
4.3
), we conclude that

\{e^{1/(m(n)}-e^{1/l(n)}\},\,\{e^{1/2l(n)}-e^{1/l(n)}\}\in N_{cu}.

It follows that
p=\Pi(\{e^{1/l(n)}\})
is a projection and
\{e^{1/l(n)}\}
is a permanent projection lifting.

Let
\{b_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}(A).

Then, for each
n\in\mathbb{N},
there is
m(n)\in\mathbb{N}
with
m(n)\geq l(n)
such that

\|e^{1/m(n)}b_{n}-b_{n}\|<1/n.
Recall that
p=\Pi(\{e^{1/m(n)}\}).

Thus
p\Pi(\{b_{n}\})=\Pi(\{b_{n}\}).
It follows
l^{\infty}(A)/{{N_{cu}}}

is unital.

Conversely, let
p\in l^{\infty}(A)/{{N_{cu}}}

be the unit. Let
\{e_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}(A)_{+}^{\bf 1}

such that
\Pi(\{e_{n}\})=p.
We claim that
\Pi(A)^{\perp}=\{0\}.

Otherwise, for each
n,
there exists
b_{n}\in A_{+}
with
\|b_{n}\|=1

such that
\|e_{n}b_{n}\|<1/n.
Then

p\Pi(\{b_{n}\})=0.
Impossible. Thus
\Pi(A)^{\perp}=\{0\}.
By Proposition
2.20
,

A
has continuous scale.
∎

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
A
be a compact

C^{*}
-algebra with

QT(A)\not=\emptyset.

If
A
has
T
-tracial approximate oscillation zero, then, for
any
x\in(l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}})_{+},
there is a projection

p\in{{l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}}}
such that
px=x=xp.

Proof.

Let

B=l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}
and
\Pi:l^{\infty}(A)\to B
be the quotient map.
Let
x\in B_{+}.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that
0\leq x\leq 1.

Let
y=\{y_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}(A)
with
0\leq y\leq 1
such that
\Pi(y)=x.

Since
A
has T-tracial approximate oscillation zero,
there are
d_{n}\in\mathrm{Her}(y_{n})_{+}^{\bf 1}
and
\delta_{n}{{\in(0,1/4n)}}
such that

\displaystyle\|y_{n}-d_{n}\|_{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}<1/4n,\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,

(e 6.23)

\displaystyle d_{\tau}(d_{n})-\tau(f_{\delta_{n}}(d_{n}))<1/4n\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,n\in\mathbb{N}.

(e 6.24)

Define
d=\{d_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}(A).
Then
\Pi(d)=\Pi({{y}})=x.

Put
e_{n}=f_{\delta_{n}/2}(d_{n}),\,\,n\in\mathbb{N}
and
e=\{e_{n}\}.
Then
e\in{{l^{\infty}(A{)_{+}^{\bf 1}}.}}

Moreover

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|e_{n}d_{n}-d_{n}\|=0.

(e 6.25)

It follows that

\displaystyle{{\Pi(e)x=}}\Pi(e)\Pi(d)=\Pi(d)=x{{.}}

(e 6.26)

It remains to show that
p:=\Pi(e)
is a projection.
To do this, we compute that

\displaystyle\tau(e_{n}-e_{n}^{2})\leq\tau(e_{n}-f_{\delta_{n}}(d_{n}))<d_{\tau}(d_{n})-\tau(f_{\delta_{n}}(d_{n}))<1/n\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,n\in\mathbb{N}.

(e 6.27)

It follows that

\displaystyle\|e_{n}-e_{n}^{2}\|_{{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}}<1/\sqrt{n}\to 0.

(e 6.28)

Thus
p=\Pi(e)=\Pi(e)^{2},
or
p\in B
is a projection.
∎

Theorem 6.4
.

Let
A
be a compact

C^{*}
-algebra with
non-empty
QT(A).

If
A
has T-tracial approximate oscillation zero, then

l^{\infty}(A)/I_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}(A)
has real rank zero.

Proof.

Let

B=l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}(A)
and
\Pi:l^{\infty}(A)\to B
be the quotient map.

We claim that, if
p\in B
is a non-zero projection, then

C:=p{{\widetilde{B}}}p=pBp
has real rank zero.

Let
\{e_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}(A)_{+}^{\bf 1}
such that
\Pi(\{e_{n}\})=p.

Upon replacing
e_{n}
by

f_{1/4}(e_{n}),
by Lemma
6.2
, we may assume
that
\{e_{n}\}
is a permanent projection lifting of
p.

By (5) of
Proposition

6.2
,
C=pBp=\Pi(l^{\infty}({{\{}}{\rm Her}(e_{n}){{\}}})).

Let
a,b\in C_{+}
be such that
ab=0.

We may assume that
\|a\|,\|b\|\leq 1.

Then, by Proposition 10.1.10 of
[
31
]
, for example, we may assume that

a=\Pi(\{a_{n}\})
and
b=\Pi(\{b_{n}\}),
where
a_{n},b_{n}\in{\rm Her}(e_{n})_{+}
and
\{a_{n}\},\,\{b_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}({{\{}}{\rm Her}(e_{n}){{\}}})
such that

a_{n}b_{n}=b_{n}a_{n}=0
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}.

Since
A
has T-tracial approximate oscillation zero, there are
d_{n}\in{\rm Her}(a_{n})_{+}^{\bf 1}
and
\delta_{n}{{\in(0,1/4n)}}
such that

\displaystyle\|a_{n}-d_{n}\|_{{{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}}}<1/4n\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,

(e 6.29)

\displaystyle|d_{\tau}(d_{n})-\tau(f_{\delta_{n}}(d_{n}))|<1/4n\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,n\in\mathbb{N}.

(e 6.30)

Define
d=\{d_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}({{\{}}{\rm Her}(a_{n}){{\}}}).
Then
a=\Pi(\{a_{n}\})=\Pi(d).

Put

\displaystyle{{g_{n}=f_{\delta_{n}/2}(d_{n}),\,\,n\in\mathbb{N}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,g^{\prime}:=\{g_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}({{\{}}{\rm Her}(a_{n}){{\}}})\subset l^{\infty}({{\{}}{\rm Her}(e_{n}){{\}}})\subset l^{\infty}(A).}}

(e 6.31)

Put
{{g=\Pi(g^{\prime})}}.

Recall that
l^{\infty}({{\{}}{\rm Her}(e_{n}){{\}}})=pBp.
Thus
g\in pBp_{+}.
Since
d_{n}\in{\rm Her}(a_{n}),
we have
g_{n}b_{n}=b_{n}g_{n}=0.

In other words,

\displaystyle gb=0.

(e 6.32)

Note that
f_{\delta_{n}}(d_{n})g_{n}=f_{\delta_{n}}(d_{n})
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}.

It follows that

\displaystyle g_{n}^{2}\geq f_{\delta_{n}}(d_{n})\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,n\in\mathbb{N}.

(e 6.33)

We compute that

\displaystyle\tau(g_{n}-g_{n}^{2})\leq\tau(g_{n}-f_{\delta_{n}}(d_{n}))<d_{\tau}(d_{n})-\tau(f_{\delta_{n}}(d_{n}))<1/n\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,n\in\mathbb{N}.

(e 6.34)

It follows that

\displaystyle\|g_{n}-g_{n}^{2}\|_{{{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}}}<1/\sqrt{n}\to 0.

(e 6.35)

Thus
{{g=g^{2}}},
or
g\in pBp
is a projection.
Recall that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|{{g_{n}}}d_{n}-d_{n}\|=\lim_{n\to\infty}\|f_{\delta_{n}/2}(d_{n})d_{n}-d_{n}\|=0.

(e 6.36)

In other words,

\displaystyle{{g}}a=g\Pi(d)=\Pi(d)=a.

(e 6.37)

This and (
e 6.32
)
imply that
pBp
has real rank zero and the claim is proved.

To show that
B
has real rank zero,
let
x\in B_{s.a.}
and let
\varepsilon>0.

Put
z=x^{2}.

Then, by Lemma
6.3
, there is a projection

p\in B
such that
pz=z=zp.

Hence
x\in pBp.

By the claim that we have just shown,
pBp
has real rank zero.
Then there is
y\in(pBp)_{s.a.}
with finite spectrum
such that
\|x-y\|<\varepsilon.
By Theorem 2.9 of
[
5
]
,

B
has real rank zero.
∎

If
A
is unital, then
l^{\infty}(A)
is the multiplier algebra of
c_{0}(A).

Thus
l^{\infty}(A)/c_{0}(A)
is a corona algebra. Therefore
l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}
is
a SSAW*-algebra (see Proposition 3 of
[
34
]
and section 3 of
[
35
]
). The above theorem also implies
that, for non-unital case, we also have the next corollary. (One may also compare the
6.5

and
6.7

(at least in untal case) with those of Lemma 1.8, Theorem 1.9 and Corollary 1.10
of
[
23
]
.)

Corollary 6.5
.

Let
A
be a
\sigma
-unital compact
C^{*}
-algebra  with
QT(A)\not=\emptyset.

Suppose that
A
has T-tracial approximate oscillation zero.
Then
l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}
is a SSAW*-algebra with real rank zero.

Proof.

This is contained in the proof of Lemma
6.4
.
Since
M_{n}(A)
also has T-tracial approximate oscillation zero,
it suffices to show that

B:=l^{\infty}(A)/I_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}
is a SAW*-algebra.
Consider elements
a,b\in B_{+}
such that
ab=ba=0.

By Lemma
6.4
, there is a projection
p\in B
such
that

{{p(a+b)=(a+b)p=a+b.}}

Then the first part of the proof of Theorem
6.4

provides a projection
{{g}}\in B
such that
{{g}}a=a
and
{{g}}b=0.
Consequently

B
is a SAW*-algebra of real rank zero.

∎

Theorem 6.6
.

Let
A
be an algebraically simple
C^{*}
-algebra
with
QT(A)\not=\emptyset.

Suppose that
A
has strict comparison and T-tracial approximate oscillation zero. Then
l^{\infty}(A)/I_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}
has stable rank one.

Proof.

Put
B=l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}}}.

We first show that
pBp
has stable rank one if
p
is a non-zero projection.
By Theorem
6.4
,
pBp
has real rank zero.
Let
q,f\in pBp
be projections such that
q\sim f.
Then, by Theorem
3.5
,

p-q\sim p-f.
By Proposition III 2.4 of
[
2
]
and Theorem 2.6 of
[
5
]
,
pBp
has stable rank one.

To show
B
has stable rank one, let
x\in{{\widetilde{B}}}
and
\varepsilon>0.

Write
x=\lambda+y,
where
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
and
y\in B.

By Lemma
6.3
, there is a projection
p\in B
such that

p(y^{*}y+yy^{*})=(y^{*}y+yy^{*}).
It follows that
y\in pBp.

From what has been shown,
pBp
has stable rank one. Choose
z_{1}\in GL(pBp)

such that

\displaystyle\|\lambda p+y-z_{1}\|<\varepsilon.

(e 6.38)

Define
z_{2}=z_{1}+\lambda(1-p),
if
\lambda\not=0,
and
z_{2}=z_{1}+\varepsilon(1-p),
if
\lambda=0.

Then
z_{2}\in GL({{\widetilde{B}}})
and

\displaystyle\|x-z_{2}\|<\varepsilon.

∎

Corollary 6.7
.

Let
A
be an algebraically simple
C^{*}
-algebra
with
QT(A)\not=\emptyset.

Suppose that
A
has strict comparison and T-tracial approximate oscillation zero. Then
l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}
has
unitary polar decomposition.

Proof.

This follows from Corollary
6.5
above and Theorem 3.5 of
[
35
]
.
∎

7
Range of dimension functions

In this section we will show that if
A
has T-tracial approximate oscillation zero,
then the image of
\Gamma
is “dense”, and if, in addition,
A
has strict comparison,
then
\Gamma
is surjective.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
0\leq a\leq 1,

b,c\in A^{\bf 1}

be such that

\displaystyle a\leq(b+c)^{*}(b+c)

(e 7.1)

(or
a\leq b+c,

b,c\in A_{+}^{\bf 1}
).
Then, for any
\delta\in(0,1/2),

\displaystyle[f_{\delta}(a)]\leq[f_{\delta/4}(b^{*}b)]+[f_{\delta/4}(c^{*}c)]

(e 7.2)

(or
[f_{\delta}(a)]\leq[f_{\delta/4}(b)]+[f_{\delta/4}(c)]
).

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle(b+c)^{*}(b+c)\leq 2(b^{*}b+c^{*}c).

(e 7.3)

Let
0<\eta<1/4.

Then, by Lemma 1.7 of
[
37
]
,

\displaystyle((b+c)^{*}(b+c)-\delta/2)_{+}\lesssim(2(b^{*}b+c^{*}c)-\delta/2)_{+}\lesssim f_{\delta/2}(b^{*}b+c^{*}c)

(e 7.4)

(recall
2.5
).
Put

z=\begin{pmatrix}b&0\\
c&0\end{pmatrix}.

Then

{{z^{*}z={\rm diag}(b^{*}b+c^{*}c,0)\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,zz^{*}\leq 2{\rm diag}(bb^{*},cc^{*}).}}

Hence (see Lemma 1.7 of
[
37
]
, for example, for the first
``\lesssim"
sign below),

\displaystyle f_{\delta/2}(b^{*}b+c^{*}c)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle f_{\delta/2}(zz^{*})\sim(zz^{*}-\delta/4)_{+}

(e 7.5)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle{\rm diag}(f_{\delta/4}(bb^{*}),f_{\delta/4}(cc^{*}))\sim{\rm diag}(f_{\delta/4}(b^{*}b),f_{\delta/4}(c^{*}c)).

(e 7.6)

We then have (see also (
e 7.4
))

\displaystyle f_{\delta}(a)\sim(a-\delta/2)_{+}\lesssim((b+c)^{*}(b+c)-\delta/2)_{+}\lesssim{\rm diag}(f_{\delta/4}(b^{*}b),f_{\delta/4}(c^{*}c)).

(e 7.7)

For the case that
a\leq b+c,
as computed above,
there is
z\in M_{2}(A)
such that

\displaystyle z^{*}z={\rm diag}(b+c,0)\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,zz^{*}\leq 2{\rm diag}(b,c).

(e 7.8)

One then sees the proof of the second part is exactly the same as that of the first part.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
A
be a non-elementary simple
C^{*}
-algebra  with

{\rm Ped}(A)=A
and
with
QT(A)\not=\emptyset.

Let
\{e_{n}\},\{b_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}({\rm Ped}(A){{{}_{+}^{\bf 1}}}).

Recall that
\Pi:l^{\infty}(A)\to l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}
is the quotient map.

(1) Suppose
that
\Pi(\{e_{n}\})\leq\Pi(\{b_{n}\})
(or
\Pi_{cu}(\{e_{n}\})\leq\Pi_{cu}(\{b_{n}\})
). Then,
any integer
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varepsilon>0,
(any
d\in{\rm Ped}(A)_{+}^{\bf 1}\setminus\{0\}
),
there exists
k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for all
k\geq k_{0},

\displaystyle[f_{\varepsilon}(e_{k})]\leq[b_{k}]+[d_{k}],

(e 7.9)

where

d_{k}\in A_{+}
and

\sup\{d_{\tau}(d_{k}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<1/m
(or
d_{k}\lesssim d
and
\sup\{d_{\tau}(d_{k}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<1/m
).

(2) Suppose that
p=\Pi(\{e_{n}\})
is a projection (or
p=\Pi_{cu}(\{e_{n}\})
is a projection
and
\omega^{c}(e_{n})\to 0
),

\{e_{n}\}
is a permanent projection lifting of
p

and

\displaystyle p\leq\Pi(\{b_{n}\})\,\,\,\,{\rm{(or}}\,\,p\leq\Pi_{cu}(\{b_{n}\}){\rm)}.

(e 7.10)

Then,
any integer
m\in\mathbb{N},
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
(and any nonzero
d\in{\rm Ped}(A)_{+}
),
there exists
k_{0}\geq\mathbb{N}
such that, for all
k\geq k_{0},

\displaystyle[e_{k}]\leq[b_{k}]+[d_{k}],

(e 7.11)

where
0\leq d_{k}\leq 1,

d_{k}\in(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}
and

\displaystyle d_{\tau}(e_{k})<d_{\tau}(b_{k})+d_{\tau}(d_{k})+\varepsilon\,\,\,\text{for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w},

(e 7.12)

where
\sup\{d_{\tau}(d_{k}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<1/m,

(or,

d_{k}\lesssim d
and
\sup\{d_{\tau}(d_{k}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<1/m
).

Proof.

We will use the following easy claim: If
B
is a
C^{*}
-algebra  and
I\subset B
is a (closed two-sided) ideal,
and, if
x,y\in B_{+}
such that
\pi(x)\leq\pi(y),
then there exists

j\in I_{+}
such that
x\leq y+j.

In fact, there is
z\in A_{+}
such that
(y-x)-z\in I.
Put
c=-y+x+z\in I.

Then

{{x=y-z+c\leq y+c\leq y+c_{+}.}}

Choose
j=c_{+}\in I_{+}.
This proves the claim.

Since
A
is a non-elementary simple
C^{*}
-algebra, one may choose
d_{0}\in{\rm Her}(d)_{+}^{\bf 1}{{\setminus\{0\}}}
such that

2(m+1)[d_{0}]\leq[d].

By the easy claim above,
there is

\{h_{n}\}\in(I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}})_{+}^{\bf 1}
(or
\{h_{n}\}\in N_{cu}(A)_{+}^{\bf 1}
) such that,
in all cases,

\displaystyle b_{n}+h_{n}\geq e_{n}\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,n\in\mathbb{N}.

(e 7.13)

To show (1), we apply
Lemma
7.1
to obtain

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}(e_{n})\lesssim{\rm diag}(f_{\varepsilon/8}(b_{n}),f_{\varepsilon/8}(h_{n})).

(e 7.14)

Since
h_{n}
is in
(I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}})_{+}

(or in
(N_{cu})_{+}
),
there exists
k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such
that, for all
k\geq k_{0},

\displaystyle d_{\tau}(f_{\varepsilon/8}(h_{k}))<1/m\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}

(e 7.15)

(or
f_{\varepsilon/8}((h_{k})_{+})\lesssim d_{0}
).
Therefore, with
d_{k}=f_{\varepsilon/8}(h_{k}),
for all
k\geq k_{0},

\displaystyle[f_{\varepsilon}(e_{k})]\leq[b_{k}]+[d_{k}]\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,\sup\{d_{\tau}(d_{k}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<1/m.

(e 7.16)

Part (1) follows.

For (2), we keep the same
d_{k}
and
h_{k}
as described above.
Since now
\{e_{n}\}
is a permanent projection lifting,
we may assume that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n})=0,

by (2) of Proposition
6.2
.
Thus we may assume that there exists
\eta_{0}>0
and
n_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\sup\{d_{\tau}(g_{\eta}(e_{n})):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}\leq\sup\{d_{\tau}(g_{\eta_{0}}(e_{n})):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<\varepsilon/2m

(e 7.17)

(see
2.5
) for all

n\geq n_{0}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,0<\eta\leq\eta_{0},

(or we assume that
\omega^{c}(e_{n})\to 0,
and then

\displaystyle g_{\eta}(e_{n})\lesssim g_{\eta_{0}}(e_{n})\lesssim d_{0}\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,n\geq n_{0}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,0<\eta\leq\eta_{0}.

(e 7.18)

for all

n\geq n_{0}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,0<\eta\leq\eta_{0}
).

There exists
n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such
that (recall that
h_{n}\in(I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}})^{\bf 1}_{+}
) (or
h_{n}\in(N_{cu})^{\bf 1}_{+}
)

\displaystyle d_{\tau}(f_{\eta_{0}/8}((h_{n})))<\varepsilon/2m\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,n\geq n_{2}

(e 7.19)

(or

f_{\eta_{0}/8}((h_{n}))\lesssim d_{0}

for
n\geq n_{2}
).
We have, by Lemma
7.1
,

\displaystyle f_{\eta_{0}/2}(e_{n})\lesssim{\rm diag}(f_{\eta_{0}/8}(b_{n}),f_{\eta_{0}/8}((h_{n}))).

(e 7.20)

Put
k_{0}=\max\{n_{1},n_{2}\}.

Then, if
n\geq k_{0},

we have

\displaystyle[e_{n}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle[g_{\eta_{0}}(e_{n})]+[f_{\eta_{0}/2}(e_{n})]\leq[g_{\eta_{0}}(e_{n})]+[f_{\eta_{0}/8}(b_{n})]+[f_{\eta_{0}/8}((h_{n}))]

(e 7.21)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle[b_{n}]+[d_{n}],

(e 7.22)

where
d_{n}={\rm diag}(g_{\eta_{0}}(e_{n}),f_{\eta_{0}/8}((h_{n}))),

n\in\mathbb{N}.

Recall (by (
e 7.17
) and (
e 7.19
)) that
d_{\tau}(d_{n})<1/m
for all
\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}

(or, in the second case,

[d_{k}]\leq 2[d_{0}]\leq[d]
).
Part (2) then follows.
∎

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
A
be a non-elementary algebraically simple
C^{*}
-algebra
with
QT(A)\not=\emptyset.

(1) Suppose that
A
has tracial approximate oscillation zero.
Then, for any
a\in A_{+}^{\bf 1}\setminus\{0\},

there exists a sequence
0\leq e_{n}\leq 1
in
{\rm Her}(a)
such
that
\Pi_{cu}(\{e_{n}\})
is full in
l^{\infty}(A)/N_{cu}(A)
and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n})=0.

(e 7.23)

(2) Suppose that
A
has
T
-tracial approximate oscillation zero.
Then, for any
a\in A_{+}^{\bf 1}\setminus\{0\},

there exists a sequence
0\leq e_{n}\leq 1
in
{\rm Her}(a)
such
that
\Pi_{cu}(\{e_{n}\})
is full in
l^{\infty}(A)/N_{cu}(A)
and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n})=0.

(e 7.24)

Proof.

(1) Since
A
has tracial approximate oscillation zero, by Proposition
5.6
, there exists a tracial approximate identity

\{e_{n}\}
for
{\rm Her}(a)
(with
\|e_{n}\|\leq 1
) such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n})=0.

Note
that

\displaystyle\Pi_{cu}(\iota(a))=\Pi_{cu}(\iota(a^{1/2})\{e_{n}\}\iota(a^{1/2})).

(e 7.25)

Since
A={\rm Ped}(A),
by Proposition 5.6 of
[
10
]
,

\iota(a)
is full in
l^{\infty}(A).
Hence

\Pi_{cu}(\iota(a))
is full in
l^{\infty}(A)/N_{cu}(A),
and so is
\Pi(\{e_{n}\}).

The proof of (2) is exactly the same. We omit it.
∎

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
A
be a non-elementary algebraically simple
C^{*}
-algebra
with
QT(A)\not=\emptyset.

Suppose that

A
has T- tracial approximate oscillation zero.
Then, for any
n\in\mathbb{N},
there is a full projection
p\in l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}

with
p=\Pi(\{e_{j}\})
for some
e_{j}\in A_{+}^{\bf 1}
(
j\in\mathbb{N}
)
such that

\displaystyle\sup\{d_{\tau}(e_{j}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<1/(n+1)\,\,\,\text{for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,j\in\mathbb{N},

(e 7.26)

and there is a sequence of mutually orthogonal full projections
p_{1},p_{2},...p_{k},...

in
l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}
such that

pp_{j}=0,

j\in\mathbb{N}
and

\displaystyle 2^{2k}[p_{k}]\leq[p],\,\,k\in\mathbb{N}.

(e 7.27)

Moreover, for each
k\in\mathbb{N},
there are mutually orthogonal and
mutually equivalent full projections

p_{k,1},p_{k,2},...,p_{k,2^{k+1}}
in
p_{k}(l^{\infty}(A)/I_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}})p_{k}.

Proof.

Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}.
Since
A
is a non-elementary simple
C^{*}
-algebra, we may choose
two mutually orthogonal
elements
a_{1},a_{2}\in{\rm Ped}(A)_{+}^{\bf 1}\setminus\{0\}

and
x\in A_{+}

such that

\displaystyle x^{*}x=a_{1},\,\,xx^{*}=a_{2}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,\sup\{d_{\tau}(a_{1}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<1/(n+1).

(e 7.28)

Find four mutually orthogonal and mutually Cuntz equivalent elements

a_{2,1},...,a_{2,4}\in{\rm Her}(a_{2})_{+}^{\bf 1}\setminus\{0\}.

By Lemma
7.3
, there exists a sequence
\{e_{n}^{\prime}\}
in

{\rm Her}(a_{1})^{\bf 1}

such that
\Pi(\{e_{n}^{\prime}\})
is full and
\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n}^{\prime})=0.

There exists, by (3) of Lemma
6.2
, a sequence of integers
m(j)
such
that
p=\Pi(\{e_{j}^{\prime})^{1/m(j)}\})
is a full projection.
Note that

\displaystyle\sup\{\tau(e_{j}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<1/(n+1),

(e 7.29)

where
e_{j}=(e_{j}^{\prime})^{1/m(j)},

j\in\mathbb{N}.

In
B={\rm Her}(a_{2,1}),
one finds a sequence of mutually orthogonal non-zero positive elements
\{y_{n}\}

such that

\displaystyle 2^{2k}[y_{k+1}]\leq[y_{k}],\,\,\,k\in\mathbb{N}.

(e 7.30)

To see this, choose mutually orthogonal nonzero elements

b_{1},b_{1}^{\prime}\in B_{+}^{\bf 1}
and
c_{1}\in B
such
that
c_{1}^{*}c_{1}=b_{1}
and
c_{1}c_{1}^{*}=b_{1}^{\prime}.

Choose
y_{1}=b_{1}.
There are mutually orthogonal and mutually Cuntz equivalent
nonzero elements
b_{2,i}\in\mathrm{Her}(b_{1}^{\prime})
(
1\leq i\leq 2^{2}
).
Choose
y_{2}=b_{2,1}.

We then proceed to divide
b_{2,4}.
A standard induction argument
produces the desired sequence
\{y_{k}\}.

For each
k,
there are
2^{k+1}
mutually orthogonal non-zero elements
y_{k,1},y_{k,2},...,y_{k,2^{k+1}}

in
\mathrm{Her}(y_{k})

and elements

z_{k,1},z_{k,2},...,z_{k,2^{k+1}}
in
\mathrm{Her}(y_{k})
such
that

\displaystyle z_{k,j}^{*}z_{k,j}=y_{k,1}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,z_{k,j}z_{k,j}^{*}=y_{k,j},\,\,\,1\leq j\leq 2^{k+1}.

(e 7.31)

Applying Lemma
7.3
, one obtains, for each
k\in\mathbb{N},

a sequence
\{e_{k,1,n}\}\subset{\rm Her}(y_{k,1})_{+}^{\bf 1}

such that
\Pi(\{e_{k,1,n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}})
is full in

l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}
and
\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{k,1,n})=0.

Since
\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{k,1,n})=0,
by (3) of Lemma
6.2
,
there is also, for each
k,
a sequence
m(n,k)\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle p_{k,1}:=\Pi(\{e_{k,1,n}^{1/m(n,k)}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}})

(e 7.32)

is a full projection in
l^{\infty}(A)/I_{S,0}.

Write
z_{k,j}=u_{k,j}|z_{k,j}|
as polar decomposition of
z_{k,j}
in
A^{**},

(
1\leq j\leq 2^{k+1}
).
Put

\displaystyle{{v_{k,j}=\Pi(\{u_{k,j}e_{k,1,n}^{1/m(n,k)}\})\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,p_{k,j}=v_{k,j}v_{k,j}^{*},\,\,1\leq j\leq 2^{k+1}.}}

(e 7.33)

Then
v_{k,j}^{*}v_{k,j}=p_{k,1}
(see (
e 7.31
)).
Thus we obtains mutually orthogonal and mutually equivalent
full projections
p_{k,j},

j=1,2,...,2^{k+1}.

By the construction, we also have
p_{k,j}p_{k^{\prime},j^{\prime}}=0,
if
k\not=k^{\prime},
as well as

pp_{k,j}=0
for all
k,j\in N.

Put
p_{k}:=\sum_{j=1}^{2^{k+1}}p_{k,j}.

Note also

\displaystyle 2^{2k}[p_{k}]\leq[p].

∎

The following two lemmas are variations of S. Zhang’s halving projection lemma.
We need some modification as we do not assume the
C^{*}
-algebra  is simple.

Lemma 7.5

(S. Zhang, Theorem I.(i) of
[
54
]
)
.

Let
A
be a
C^{*}
-algebra  of real rank zero and
r
a full projection of
A.

Suppose that

p\in A
is a non-zero projection such that
[p]\not\leq[r].

Then,
there are mutually orthogonal projections
p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\in A
such that

\displaystyle p=p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3},\,\,p_{1}\sim p_{2},\,\,\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,p_{3}\lesssim r.

(e 7.34)

Proof.

We begin with the following claim which is
extracted from the proof of

[
54
, Theorem I.(i)]
.

Claim:
Let
C
be a
C^{*}
-algebra and let
v_{1},...,v_{2^{m}}\in C
be partial isometries
such that
v_{i}v_{i}^{*}\perp v_{j}v_{j}^{*}
(
i\neq j
)
and
v_{i}^{*}v_{i}\geq v_{i+1}^{*}v_{i+1}
(
1\leq i\leq 2^{m}-1
).
Then there is a partial isometry
v\in C
such that

v^{*}v\perp vv^{*}
and

0\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{i}v_{i}^{*}-(v^{*}v+vv^{*})\leq v_{1}v_{1}^{*}.

Proof of the claim:
We use induction on
m.

When
m=1,
let
v:=v_{1}v_{2}^{*}.
Then
v^{*}v\perp vv^{*}

because
v_{1}v_{1}^{*}\perp v_{2}v_{2}^{*},
and
v^{*}v=v_{2}v_{1}^{*}v_{1}v_{2}^{*}=v_{2}v_{2}^{*}

because
v_{1}^{*}v_{1}\geq v_{2}^{*}v_{2}.

Note that

vv^{*}=v_{1}v_{2}^{*}v_{2}v_{1}^{*}.

Thus

\displaystyle v_{1}v_{1}^{*}+v_{2}v_{2}^{*}-(v^{*}v+vv^{*})=v_{1}v_{1}^{*}-v_{1}v_{2}^{*}v_{2}v_{1}^{*}=v_{1}(1-v_{2}^{*}v_{2})v_{1}^{*}\leq v_{1}v_{1}^{*}.

(e 7.35)

The last equation above also shows that

v_{1}v_{1}^{*}+v_{2}v_{2}^{*}-(v^{*}v+vv^{*})
is positive. Hence the claim holds
for
m=1.

Assume that the claim holds for
m\geq 1.

Let
v_{1},...,v_{2^{m+1}}
be partial isometries
such that
v_{i}v_{i}^{*}\perp v_{j}v_{j}^{*}
(
i\neq j
)
and
v_{i}^{*}v_{i}\geq v_{i+1}^{*}v_{i+1}
(
1\leq i\leq 2^{m+1}-1
).
Define
e_{i}:=v_{i}^{*}v_{i}
and

w_{i}=v_{i}(e_{i}-e_{2^{m+1}-i+1}),

i=1,...,2^{m}.

For
i=1,...,2^{m}-1,
we have

\displaystyle w_{i}^{*}w_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(e_{i}-e_{2^{m+1}-i+1})e_{i}(e_{i}-e_{2^{m+1}-i+1})

(e 7.36)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e_{i}-e_{2^{m+1}-i+1}\geq e_{i+1}-e_{2^{m+1}-i}=w_{i+1}^{*}w_{i+1}.

(e 7.37)

Note that the above
also shows that
w_{i}^{*}w_{i}
are projections for all
i.

Since
w_{i}w_{i}^{*}\leq v_{i}v_{i}^{*},
we have that
w_{i}w_{i}^{*}
are mutually orthogonal.

Consider
w_{i},

1\leq i\leq 2^{m}.

By induction, there is a partial isometry
w\in C
such that

w^{*}w\perp ww^{*}
and

\displaystyle 0\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}w_{i}w_{i}^{*}-(w^{*}w+ww^{*})\leq w_{1}w_{1}^{*}\ (\leq v_{1}v_{1}^{*}).

(e 7.38)

Hence
ww=0.

Note that

\displaystyle w^{*}w+ww^{*}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}w_{i}w_{i}^{*}=\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{i}(e_{i}-e_{2^{m+1}-i+1})v_{i}^{*}

(e 7.39)

\displaystyle\hskip 21.68121pt=\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{i}v_{i}^{*}-v_{i}e_{2^{m+1}-i+1}v_{i}^{*}\in\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{i}e_{2^{m+1}-i+1}v_{i}^{*}\right)^{\perp}\cap\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*}\right)^{\perp},

where
b^{\perp}=\{a\in A:ab=ba=0\}.

Recall that
v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}=e_{2^{m+1}-i+1}
(
1\leq i\leq 2^{m}
) and

v_{i}^{*}v_{i}\geq v_{i+1}^{*}v_{i+1}
(
1\leq i\leq 2^{m+1}-1
).
Hence

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{i}e_{2^{m+1}-i+1}v_{i}^{*}=\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{i}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*}(v_{i}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*})^{*}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,

(e 7.40)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*}=\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}(v_{i}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*})^{*}v_{i}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*}.

(e 7.41)

Thus

\displaystyle\hskip 14.45377ptww^{*}+w^{*}w\in\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{i}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*}(v_{i}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*})^{*}\right)^{\perp}\cap\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}(v_{i}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*})^{*}v_{i}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*}\right)^{\perp}.

It follows that, for
1\leq i\leq 2^{m},

\displaystyle w\perp v_{i}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*}.

(e 7.42)

Define

v:=w+\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{i}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*}.

By (
e 7.42
) and the fact that

w^{2}=0
together with

v_{i}v_{i}^{*}\perp v_{j}v_{j}^{*}
(
i\neq j
), we compute that

\displaystyle\hskip-28.90755ptv^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(w+\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{i}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*})(w+\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{i}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*})=0,

(e 7.43)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.90755ptv^{*}v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w^{*}w+\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}v_{i}^{*}v_{i}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*}=w^{*}w+\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*},\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,

(e 7.44)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.90755ptvv^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle ww^{*}+\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{i}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}^{*}v_{2^{m+1}-i+1}v_{i}^{*}=ww^{*}+\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{i}e_{2^{m+1}-i+1}v_{i}^{*}.

(e 7.45)

Then

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m+1}}v_{i}v_{i}^{*}-(v^{*}v+vv^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{i}v_{i}^{*}-\left(w^{*}w+ww^{*}+\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}v_{i}e_{2^{m+1}-i+1}v_{i}^{*}\right)

(e 7.46)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}\left(v_{i}v_{i}^{*}-v_{i}e_{2^{m+1}-i+1}v_{i}^{*}\right)-\left(w^{*}w+ww^{*}\right)

(e 7.47)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{0116-4}}{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}w_{i}w_{i}^{*}-\left(w^{*}w+ww^{*}\right)

(e 7.48)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{0116-5}}{\leq}

\displaystyle w_{1}w_{1}^{*}\leq v_{1}v_{1}^{*}.

(e 7.49)

By induction, the claim holds for all
m\in\mathbb{N}.

For the proof of the lemma,
applying Lemma 1.1 of
[
52
]
, we obtain partial isometries

v_{1},v_{2},...,v_{n}\in A
such that

\displaystyle r\geq v_{1}^{*}v_{1}\geq v_{2}^{*}v_{2}\geq\cdots v_{n}^{*}v_{n}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,

(e 7.50)

\displaystyle p=v_{1}v_{1}^{*}\oplus v_{2}v_{2}^{*}\oplus\cdots\oplus v_{n}v_{n}^{*}.

(e 7.51)

Since
[p]\not\leq[r],

n\geq 2.
By adding
0
if necessary,
we may assume that
n=2^{m}
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}.

Then by (
e 7.50
), (
e 7.51
), and the claim,
there is a partial isometry
v\in A
such that

v^{*}v\perp vv^{*},

0\leq p-(v^{*}v+vv^{*})\leq v_{1}v_{1}^{*}\lesssim r.

Then the lemma holds (by choosing
p_{1}=vv^{*}
and
p_{2}=v^{*}v
).
∎

Lemma 7.6

(S. Zhang)
.

Let
A
be a
C^{*}
-algebra  of real rank zero and
r
be a full projection of
A

such that
B=(1-r)A(1-r)
contains, for each
k\in\mathbb{N},

a sequence of mutually orthogonal
full projections
\{r_{n,j}^{\prime}:1\leq j\leq 2^{n+1},n\in\mathbb{N}\}

such that
2^{k+n}[r_{n}^{\prime}]\leq[r],

where

r_{n}^{\prime}=\sum_{j=1}^{2^{n+1}}r_{n,j}^{\prime},

and
r_{n,1}^{\prime},r_{n,2}^{\prime},...,r_{n,2^{n+1}}^{\prime}

are mutually equivalent (
n\in\mathbb{N}
).
Suppose that

p\in A
is a non-zero projection such that
[p]\not\leq[r].

Then, for any
m\in\mathbb{N},
there are mutually orthogonal projections
p_{{}_{1}},p_{{}_{2}},...,p_{{}_{2^{m}}},p_{{}_{2^{m}+1}}\in A
such that

\displaystyle p=\sum_{j=1}^{2^{m}}p_{j}+p_{{}_{2^{m}+1}},\,\,p_{{}_{j}}\sim p_{{}_{1}},1\leq j\leq 2^{m},\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,p_{{}_{2^{m}+1}}\lesssim r+r^{\prime},

(e 7.52)

where

r^{\prime}
is a finite sum of
r_{n,j}^{\prime}s
and

2[r^{\prime}]<r.

Proof.

We use the induction on
m.

If
m=1,
the lemma follows from Lemma
7.5
.

Suppose that the lemma holds for
m{{\geq 1}}.

Then
there are mutually orthogonal projections
p_{{}_{1}},p_{{}_{2}},...,p_{{}_{2^{m}}},p_{{}_{2^{m}+1}}\in A
such that

\displaystyle p=\sum_{j=1}^{2^{m}}p_{j}+p_{{}_{2^{m}+1}},\,\,p_{j}\sim p_{1},1\leq j\leq 2^{m},\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,p_{2^{m}+1}\lesssim r+r^{\prime\prime},

(e 7.53)

where

r^{\prime\prime}
is a finite sum of
r_{n,i}^{\prime}s
and

2[r^{\prime\prime}]<[r].

Choose
r_{K,1}^{\prime}
among
\{r_{n,j}^{\prime}\}
but not those which have been used
for the sum of
r^{\prime\prime}.

We choose
K
such that
K>2m.

Note that

{{2{{[r^{\prime\prime}]}}+2^{{{K}}+1}[r_{K,1}^{\prime}]<[r].}}

We also note that
[p_{1}]\not\leq[r_{K,1}^{\prime}].
(Otherwise,

[p]\leq 2^{m}[r_{K,1}^{\prime}]\leq 2^{K}[r_{K,1}]\leq[r],
a contradiction.)
Applying Lemma
7.5
to
p_{1}
(as
p
) and the full projection
r_{K,1}^{\prime}.

Then
we may write

\displaystyle p_{1}=p_{1,1}+p_{1,2}+p_{1,3},\,\,p_{1,1}\sim p_{1,2}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,p_{1,3}\lesssim r_{K,1}^{\prime}.

(e 7.54)

Since
p_{j}\sim p_{1},
we also have mutually orthogonal projections
p_{j,1},p_{j,2},p_{j,3}

such that

\displaystyle p_{j}=p_{j,1}+p_{j,2}+p_{j,3},\,\,p_{j,2}\sim p_{j,1}{{\sim p_{1,1}}}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,p_{j,3}\lesssim r_{K,1}^{\prime}.

(e 7.55)

Note that

\displaystyle p_{j,i}\sim p_{j^{\prime},i^{\prime}},\,\,i,i^{\prime}=1,2,\,\,j=1,2,....,2^{m}.

(e 7.56)

Put
s:=\sum_{j=1}^{{{2^{m}}}}p_{{}_{j,3}}.
Then

\displaystyle s\lesssim r+r^{\prime\prime}+\sum_{j=1}^{2^{{m}}}r_{K,j}^{\prime},\,\,2[r^{\prime\prime}+\sum_{j=1}^{2^{{m}}}r^{\prime}_{K,j}]\leq[r]\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,p=\sum_{j=1}^{2^{m}}(p_{j,1}+p_{j,2})+s.

(e 7.57)

This completes the induction.
∎

Recall that, if
C_{n}\subset A
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
), then
l^{\infty}(\{C_{n}\})=\{\{c_{n}\}\in l^{\infty}(A):c_{n}\in C_{n}\}.

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
A
be a non-elementary,
\sigma
-unital and algebraically simple
C^{*}
-algebra
with
QT(A)\not=\emptyset
and with
T
-tracial approximate oscillation zero.
Let
\{e_{k}\}
be a sequence in
A_{+}^{\bf 1}\setminus\{0\}
such that
\{e_{k}\}\not\in I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}},

and
\{e_{k}\}
is a permanent projection lifting of the projection
p=\Pi(\{e_{k}\}),
and
let
n>1
be an integer and
\varepsilon>0.

Then, there are mutually
orthogonal and mutually (Cuntz) equivalent elements

{{\{f_{k,1}\},\{f_{k,2}\},...,\{f_{k,n}\}\in l^{\infty}(\{\mathrm{Her}(e_{k})\})\,\,\,{\text{such\,\, that}}}}

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\omega(f_{k,j})=0,\,\,1\leq j\leq n,\,\,and,

(e 7.58)

for
k\geq k_{0}
(for some
k_{0}
),

\displaystyle\sup\{|d_{\tau}(e_{k})-nd_{\tau}(f_{k,1})|:\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<\varepsilon.

(e 7.59)

Proof.

Let
m\geq 1
be an integer and let
\varepsilon>0.
Put
B=l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}.

Since
A
has T-tracial oscillation zero, by
Theorem
6.4
,
B
has real rank zero.
Since

\{e_{k}\}\not\in I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}},

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}:=\limsup_{k}(\sup\{\tau(e_{k}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\})>0.

(e 7.60)

If
r=\Pi(\{r_{n}\})
(is full)
and
r_{n}\in A_{+}^{\bf 1},
and

\displaystyle\sup\{\tau(r_{n}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<\sigma_{0}/4\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,n\in\mathbb{N},

(e 7.61)

then
[\Pi(\{e_{k}\})]\not\leq[r].

It then follows from Lemma
7.4
and Lemma
7.6
that, there
are
2^{m}+1
mutually orthogonal projections

p_{1},p_{2},...,p_{2^{m}},s\in B
such that
s
is full,
s=\Pi(\{s_{n}\}),

where
s_{n}\in A_{+}^{\bf 1},

\displaystyle p=\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}}p_{i}+s,\,\,p_{1}\sim p_{j},1\leq j\leq 2^{m}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,

(e 7.62)

\displaystyle\sup\{d_{\tau}(s_{n}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<\varepsilon/2.

(e 7.63)

Recall that
\{e_{{k}}\}
is a permanent projection lifting of
p.

Then, by (5) of Proposition
6.2
,
pBp=l^{\infty}(\{\mathrm{Her}(e_{{k}})\})/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}.

Define a homomorphism
\varphi:C_{0}((0,1])\otimes M_{2^{m}}\to pBp
such that

\displaystyle\varphi(\imath\otimes e_{i,i})=p_{i},\,\,i=1,2,...,2^{m}.

(e 7.64)

Since
C_{0}((0,1])\otimes M_{2^{m}}
is semiprojective,
there is a homomorphism

\displaystyle{{\psi:C_{0}((0,1])\otimes M_{2^{m}}\to l^{\infty}(\{\mathrm{Her}(e_{n})\})\,\,\,{\rm such\,\,\,that}}}\,\,\,\Pi(\psi(e_{i,i}))=p_{i},\,\,i=1,2,...,2^{m}.

(e 7.65)

Write
\psi=\{\Psi_{k}\},
where
\Psi_{k}:C_{0}((0,1])\otimes M_{2^{m}}\to{\rm Her}(e_{{k}})

is a homomorphism. Put

{{g_{k,i}={{f_{1/4}}}(\Psi_{k}(e_{i,i})){{\in\mathrm{Her}(e_{k})}},\,\,\,i=1,2,...,2^{m},\,\,k\in\mathbb{N}.}}

Then
\{g_{k,1}\},\{g_{k,2}\},...,\{g_{k,2^{m}}\}
are mutually orthogonal and mutually
equivalent.
Note that
\Pi(g_{k,i})\}=p_{i}
and
\{g_{k,i}\}
is a permanent projection lifting
of
p_{i},
and by (2) of Lemma
6.2
,
\lim_{k\to\infty}\omega(g_{k,i})=0.

Then, by Lemma
7.2
, there is
k_{1}\in\mathbb{N},
such that, for all
k\geq k_{1},

\displaystyle d_{\tau}(e_{k})

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle d_{\tau}(\sum_{j=1}^{2^{m}}g_{k,j})+d_{\tau}({{s_{k}}})+\varepsilon/2\leq 2^{m}d_{\tau}(g_{k,1})+\varepsilon\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}.

(e 7.66)

Also,
by Lemma
7.2
,
we may assume that, for all
k\geq k_{1},

\displaystyle 2^{m}d_{\tau}(g_{k,1})\leq d_{\tau}(\sum_{j=1}^{2^{m}}g_{k,j}+{{s_{k}}})\leq d_{\tau}(e_{k})+\varepsilon\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}.

(e 7.67)

We choose a large
m
such that

2^{m}=ln+m_{0},
where
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
m_{0}\in{{\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}}}

such that
m_{0}/2^{m}<\varepsilon/4.

Note that, since
\{g_{k,1}\},\{g_{k,2}\},...,\{g_{k,2^{m}}\}
are mutually orthogonal,
for any sum
f_{k,j}
of some
l
many
\{g_{k,i}\}^{\prime}s,

\lim_{k\to\infty}\omega(f_{k,j})=0.

For each
1\leq j\leq n,
by (
e 7.66
) and (
e 7.67
), and
by choosing
f_{k,j}
to be a sum of
l
many (different)
g_{k,i}^{\prime}s,
we see
(
e 7.58
) and (
e 7.59
) hold.

∎

Corollary 7.8
.

Let
A
be a non-elementary,
\sigma
-unital and algebraically simple
C^{*}
-algebra  with

QT(A)\not=\emptyset
and T-tracial approximate oscillation zero.
Let
e_{k}\in A_{+}^{\bf 1}\setminus\{0\}
(
k\in\mathbb{N}
) be such that
\{e_{k}\}\not\in I_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}

and
\{e_{k}\}
is a permanent projection lifting of
p=\Pi(\{e_{k}\}),

and let
n>1
be an integer and
\varepsilon>0.

Then, there is
k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for any
k\geq k_{0}
and for any
1\leq i\leq n,
there exists
{{h}}_{k,i}\in{{\mathrm{Her}(e_{k})_{+}}}

such that

\displaystyle\sup\{|{i\over{n}}d_{\tau}(e_{k})-d_{\tau}({{h_{k,i}}})|:\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<\varepsilon.

(e 7.68)

Proof.

By the proof of Lemma
7.7
, for
k\geq k_{0},

\displaystyle\sup\{|{1\over{n}}d_{\tau}(e_{k})-d_{\tau}(f_{k,1})|:\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<\varepsilon/n.

(e 7.69)

So, for any
1\leq i\leq n,
choose
{{h_{k,i}}}=\sum_{j=1}^{i}f_{k,j}.

Then

\displaystyle\sup\{|{i\over{n}}d_{\tau}(e_{k})-d_{\tau}({{h_{k,i}}})|:\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}\}<\varepsilon.

∎

Definition 7.9
.

Let
A
be a
C^{*}
-algebra  with
{\widetilde{QT}}(A)\setminus\{0\}\not=\emptyset.

Define

\displaystyle R_{\tau,f}(A)=\{\widehat{a}:a\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}\}\subset\operatorname{Aff}_{+}({\widetilde{QT}}(A)).

(e 7.70)

Lemma 7.10
.

Let
A
be a non-elementary,
\sigma
-unital and simple
C^{*}
-algebra  with
{\widetilde{QT}}(A)\setminus\{0\}\not=\emptyset.

Suppose that
A
has
T
-tracial approximate oscillation zero.
Then the image of the canonical map
\Gamma
(see
2.13
) is dense in

R_{\tau,f}(A).

Proof.

Fix a nonzero element
0\leq e\leq 1
in
{\rm Ped}(A)_{+}.

Let
A_{1}=\mathrm{Her}(e).
Then
A_{1}={\rm Ped}(A_{1}).

By Brown’s stable isomorphism theorem (
[
4
]
),
A_{1}\otimes{\cal K}\cong A\otimes{\cal K}.

It suffices to show that the image of the map
\Gamma_{1}:\mathrm{Cu}(A_{1})=\mathrm{Cu}(A)\to{\rm LAff}_{+}(\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w})

is dense in

{{R_{\tau,f}(A_{1})=\{\hat{a}:a\in{\rm Ped}(A_{1}\otimes{\cal K})_{+}\}\subset\operatorname{Aff}_{+}(\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w})}}\hskip 28.90755pt{\rm(see\,\,\ref{DGamma}).}

Fix
a\in{\rm Ped}(A_{1}\otimes{\cal K})_{+}.

Let
\varepsilon>0.
It suffices to show that there is
f\in\mathrm{Cu}(A_{1})
such that

\displaystyle\sup\{|\tau(a)-\widehat{f}(\tau)|:\tau\in\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}\}<\varepsilon.

(e 7.71)

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
0\leq a\leq 1.

Since
a\in{\rm Ped}(A_{1}\otimes{\cal K})_{+},

there exists
r\geq 1
such that
r[f_{\delta}(e)]\geq[a]

for some
\delta\in(0,1/4).
Therefore we may assume
that
a\in M_{r}(A_{1})
for some integer
r\geq 1.

Put
B:=l^{\infty}(A_{1})/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}}}.

Then, by Theorem
6.4
,

B
has real rank zero.

Therefore, for any
\varepsilon>0,

there are mutually orthogonal projections
p_{1},p_{2},...,p_{m}\in M_{r}(B)
and

\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},...,\lambda_{m}\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\|\Pi(\iota(a))-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\lambda_{i}p_{i}\|<\varepsilon/16.

(e 7.72)

We may assume that
\lambda_{i}\in(0,1)\cap\mathbb{Q}.

There are mutually orthogonal elements
\{e_{n,i}\}\in l^{\infty}(M_{r}(A_{1}))
(
i=1,2,...,m
)
such that
\Pi(\{e_{n,i}\})=p_{i},

i=1,2,...,m.

By Proposition
6.2
, we may assume that

\{e_{n,i}\}
is a permanent projection lifting.
By (2) of Proposition
6.2
,
\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega(e_{n,i})=0.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that, for all
n\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle d_{\tau}(e_{n,i})-\tau(e_{n,i})<\varepsilon/16(m+1)^{{2}}\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in{{\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w},}}\,\,i=1,2,...,m.

(e 7.73)

Applying Corollary
7.8
, without loss of generality, we may also assume that,
there are permanent projection liftings
\{f_{n,i}\}
such that

\displaystyle\sup\{|\lambda_{i}d_{\tau}(e_{n,i})-d_{\tau}(f_{n,i})|:\tau\in{{\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}}}\}<\varepsilon/16(m+1)^{{2}},\,\,i=1,2,...,m.

(e 7.74)

By (
e 7.72
), there exists
\{c_{n}\}\in I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}}}
and
n_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for all
n\geq n_{1},

\displaystyle\|a-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\lambda_{i}e_{i,n}+c_{n}\|<\varepsilon/8.

(e 7.75)

Then, for
n\geq n_{1},

{{\sup\{|\tau(a-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\lambda_{i}e_{n,i}+c_{n})|:\tau\in\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}\}<\varepsilon/8.}}

Since
\{c_{n}\}\in I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}}},
we have
\{|c_{n}|^{1/2}\}\in I_{{{}_{\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}}}}.

It follows that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup\{|\tau(c_{n})|:\tau\in{{\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}}}\}=0.

Therefore there exists
n_{2}\geq n_{1}
such that

\displaystyle|\tau(a-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\lambda_{i}e_{n,i})|<|\tau(c_{n})|+\varepsilon/8<\varepsilon/4\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,n\geq n_{2}.

(e 7.76)

Thus, by (
e 7.73
) and (
e 7.74
),
for
n\geq n_{2},

\displaystyle|\tau(a)-\sum_{i=1}^{m}d_{\tau}(f_{n,i})|<\varepsilon/2\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}.

(e 7.77)

Put
e={\rm diag}(f_{n,1},f_{n,2},...,f_{n,m}).
Then

\displaystyle|\tau(a)-d_{\tau}(e)|<\varepsilon\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}.

(e 7.78)

This completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 7.11
.

Let
A
be a non-elementary and
\sigma
-unital simple
C^{*}
-algebra  with
\widetilde{QT}(A)\setminus\{0\}\not=\emptyset

and strict comparison.
Suppose that
A
has T-tracial approximate oscillation zero.
Then
\Gamma
is surjective (see
2.13
).

Proof.

We keep the same setting as in the proof of Theorem
7.10
.

Let
b\in{\rm Ped}(A_{1}\otimes{\cal K})_{+}
with
0\leq b\leq 1.

Choose

b_{n}=(1-1/(n+1))b.

Then
h_{n}:=b_{n+1}-b_{n}\in{\rm Ped}(A_{1}\otimes{\cal K})_{+}\setminus\{0\}.

Put

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}:=\inf\{\tau(h_{n}):\tau\in\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}\}>0.

(e 7.79)

Applying Theorem
7.10
, for each
n\in\mathbb{N},
we obtain
f_{n}\in{\rm Ped}(A_{1}\otimes{\cal K})_{+}
such that

\displaystyle\eta_{n}:=\sup\{|\tau(b_{n})-d_{\tau}(f_{n})|:\tau\in\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}\}<{\min\{\sigma_{j}:1\leq j\leq n+1\}\over{2^{n+2}}}.

(e 7.80)

In particular, for all
n\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle d_{\tau}(f_{n})<\eta_{n}+{{\tau}}(b_{n})<\tau(b)\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in{{\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}.}}

(e 7.81)

Then, for all
\tau\in\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}
(note that
b_{n}b_{n+1}=b_{n+1}b_{n}
),

\displaystyle d_{\tau}(f_{n+1})-d_{\tau}(f_{n})

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle(\tau(b_{n+1})-\eta_{n+1})-(\tau(b_{n})+\eta_{n})

(e 7.82)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{\tau}}(h_{n})-\eta_{n+1}-\eta_{n}>\tau(h_{n})-\sigma_{n}/2>0.

(e 7.83)

It follows from the strict comparison that
[f_{n}]
is an increasing sequence in
\mathrm{Cu}(A).

Let
f
be the supremum of
\{[f_{n}]\}
in
\mathrm{Cu}(A).

We also have, for all
\tau\in\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w},

\displaystyle d_{\tau}(f_{n+1})-{{\tau}}(b_{n})

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle{{\tau}}(b_{n+1})-\eta_{n+1}-{{\tau}}(b_{n})

(e 7.84)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle{{\tau}}(h_{n})-\sigma_{n}/2^{n+1}>0.

(e 7.85)

It follows that
\widehat{f}(\tau)\geq\tau(b_{n})
for all
\tau\in\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}
and for each
n\in\mathbb{N}.

Hence

{{\widehat{f}(\tau)\geq\tau(b)\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}.}}

Let
\varepsilon>0.
By Theorem
7.10
,
there is
c_{\varepsilon}\in\mathrm{Cu}(A_{1})
such that

\displaystyle\sup\{|\tau(b+(\varepsilon/2)b)-\Gamma_{1}(c_{\varepsilon})(\tau)|:\tau\in\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}\}<\varepsilon\sigma_{1}/8.

(e 7.86)

Then, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
(see also (
e 7.81
)),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}(c_{\varepsilon})(\tau)>\tau(b+\varepsilon/2b)-\varepsilon\sigma_{1}/8>\tau(b)>d_{\tau}(f_{n})\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}.

(e 7.87)

Hence
[f]\leq[c_{\varepsilon}].

It follows that

\displaystyle\widehat{f}(\tau)\leq\widehat{c_{\varepsilon}}(\tau)<\tau(b)+\varepsilon\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,\tau\in{{\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}.}}

(e 7.88)

Let
\varepsilon\to 0.
We conclude that
\widehat{f}(\tau)=\tau(b)
for all
\tau\in\overline{QT(A_{1})}^{w}.

So far we have shown that, for any
b\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+},

there is
f\in\mathrm{Cu}(A)
such that
\widehat{f}(\tau)=\tau(b)
for all
\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}.

Note that, for any
a\in(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+},

(a-\|a\|/n)_{+}\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K}).

Thus, there are
f_{n}\in\mathrm{Cu}(A)
such that
\widehat{f_{n}}(\tau)=\tau((a-\|a\|/n)_{+})
for all
\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}.

Since
(a-\|a\|/n)_{+}\nearrow a,
we conclude, using the similar argument used above,
that there is
f\in\mathrm{Cu}(A)
such that
\widehat{f}(\tau)=\tau(a)
for all
\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}.

Applying Theorem 5.7 of
[
13
]
and repeating the argument above, we conclude that

\Gamma
is surjective.
∎

8
Tracially matricial property

Definition 8.1
.

Let
A
be a

C^{*}
-algebra  and
S\subset\widetilde{QT}(A)\setminus\{0\}
be a non-empty compact subset.

C^{*}
-algebra
A
is said to have property (TM) relative to
S,

if for any
a\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+},
any
\varepsilon>0,

any
n\in\mathbb{N},

there is a c.p.c. order zero map

\varphi:M_{n}\to\mathrm{Her}(a)

such that
\|a-\varphi(1_{n})a\|_{{}_{2,S}}<\varepsilon.

A
\sigma
-unital simple
C^{*}
-algebra
A
with
\widetilde{QT}(A)\setminus\{0\}\not=\emptyset

is said to have property
(TM), if for some
e\in{\rm Ped}(A)_{+}^{\bf 1}\setminus\{0\},

\mathrm{Her}(e)
has property (TM) relative to
\overline{QT(A)}^{w}.

From the definition, it is clear that, if
A
is a
\sigma
-unital simple
C^{*}
-algebra  which has property (TM),
then
A\otimes{\cal K}
has property (TM), and
by Brown’s stable isomorphism theorem
[
4
]
,
every
\sigma
-unital hereditary
C^{*}
-subalgebra  has property (TM). Since we only
need to consider
\mathrm{Her}(a)
for each
a\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}^{\bf 1},
it follows
that every hereditary
C^{*}
-subalgebra  has property (TM).

In the absence of strict comparison, one may also define the following:

A
C^{*}
-algebra
A
is said to have property (CM), if, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and any
a\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}^{\bf 1},
there is
a c.p.c. order zero map
\varphi:M_{n}\to l^{\infty}(C)/N_{cu}(C)
such that

\displaystyle\iota(b)\varphi(1_{n})=\iota(b)\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,b\in C,

(e 8.1)

where
C=\overline{a(A\otimes{\cal K})a}.

Remark 8.2
.

In definition
8.1
, let
\psi:C_{0}((0,1])\otimes M_{n}\to\mathrm{Her}(a)

be the homomorphism  induced by
\varphi,
i.e.,
\psi(\iota\otimes e_{i,j})=\varphi(e_{i,j})
(
1\leq i,j\leq n
).
Then
\mathrm{Her}(a)
has some “tracially large” matricial structure (see Proposition
8.3
).

Let
x\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K}).
Then
a_{0}=x^{*}x+xx^{*}\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}.

Note
x\in\mathrm{Her}(a_{0}).

Let
{\cal F\subset}\mathrm{Her}(a_{0})^{\bf 1}

be a finite set.
For any
1>\varepsilon>0,

there is
a\in\mathrm{Her}(a_{0})_{+}^{\bf 1}

such that

\displaystyle\|ay-y\|<(\varepsilon/4)^{3}\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,\|ya-y\|<(\varepsilon/4)^{3}\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,y\in{\cal F}.

(e 8.2)

If
A
has property (TM) relative to
S
such that
\|\tau\|\leq 1
for all
\tau\in S,

then, there is a c.p.c. order zero map

\varphi:M_{n}\to\mathrm{Her}(a_{0})
such that

\|a-\varphi(1_{n})a\|_{{}_{2,S}}<(\varepsilon/2)^{3}.

Then, for all
y\in{\cal F},

\displaystyle\|y-\varphi(1_{n})y\|_{{}_{2,S}}^{2/3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|y-ya\|^{2/3}_{{{}_{2,S}}}+\|ya-ya\varphi(1_{n})\|_{{}_{2,S}}^{2/3}

(e 8.3)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle(\varepsilon/2)^{2}+\|y\|\|a-a\varphi(1_{n})\|_{{}_{2,S}}^{2/3}<\varepsilon.

(e 8.4)

Similarly

\displaystyle\|y-\varphi(1_{n})y\|_{{}_{2,S}}^{2/3}<\varepsilon.

(e 8.5)

The following fact is well known.
For completeness, we include a proof here.

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
A
be a
C^{*}
-algebra,
n\in\mathbb{N},

and

\varphi:M_{n}\to A
be a c.p.c. order zero map.
Then
\mathrm{Her}(\varphi(1_{n}))\cong\mathrm{Her}(\varphi(e_{1,1}))\otimes M_{n}.

Proof.

Let
\psi:C_{0}((0,1])\otimes M_{n}\to A
be the homomorphism  defined
by
\psi(\imath\otimes e_{ij})=\varphi(e_{i,j}),
where
\imath
is the identity function on
(0,1],

i,j\leq n.

In particular,
\psi(\imath\otimes 1_{n})=\varphi(1_{n}).

Write
\psi(\imath\otimes e_{i,j})=u_{i,j}r_{{j}}
as a polar decomposition of
\varphi(\imath\otimes e_{i,j})

in
A^{**}.
Hence
r_{{j}}=|{{\psi}}(\imath\otimes e_{i,j})|
and
u_{i,j}
is a partial isometry in

A^{**}.

Note that
au_{i,j}b\in A
for all
a\in p_{i}A^{**}p_{i}\cap A
and
b\in p_{j}A^{**}p_{j}\cap A,

where
p_{i}
is the open projection of
r_{i},

i,j=1,2,...,n.

Since
\psi
is a homomorphism, we compute that
p_{i}=u_{i,i},

i=1,2,...,n,
and

\{u_{i,j}\}_{1\leq i,j\leq n}
forms a system of matrix units for
M_{n}.

Define
\Phi:M_{n}({\rm Her}({{\varphi}}(e_{1,1})))\cong{\rm Her}(\varphi(e_{1,1}))\otimes M_{n}\to{\rm Her}(\varphi(1_{n}))
by
defining
\Phi(a\otimes e_{i,j})=u_{i,1}au_{1,j}
for all
a\in{\rm Her}({{\varphi}}(e_{1,1})),

i,j=1,2,...,n.

Then
\Phi
is a homomorphism. Since
\Phi
is the identity map on
{\rm Her}{{(\varphi(e_{1,1}))}}
and
M_{n}
is simple,
the map
\Phi
is injective. Put
B=\Phi(M_{n}({\rm Her}({{\varphi}}(e_{1,1})))).
To see
\Phi
is surjective, let
x\in A,

then, for any
i,j=1,2,...,n,

b_{i,j}=u_{1,i}r_{i}xr_{j}u_{j,1}\in{\rm Her}(\varphi(e_{1,1}){{)}}.
Therefore

\displaystyle\varphi(1_{n})x\varphi(1_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{{1\leq i,j\leq n}}u_{i,i}r_{i}xr_{j}u_{j,j}

(e 8.6)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq n}u_{i,1}(u_{1,i}xr_{j}u_{j,1})u_{1,j}=\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq n}u_{i,1}b_{i{{,}}j}u_{1,j}\in B.

(e 8.7)

It follows that
{\rm Her}(\varphi(1_{n}))=B.
The lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
A
be a simple
C^{*}
-algebra  with
{\widetilde{QT}}(A)\setminus\{0\}\not=\emptyset.

Suppose that
A={\rm Ped}(A),

A
has strict comparison and
\Gamma
is surjective.
Suppose that
b\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}^{\bf 1}.

Then, for any
\varepsilon>0
and any integer
n\geq 1,
there is a c.p.c. order zero map
\varphi:M_{n}\to{\rm Her}(b)
such that

\displaystyle\|b-\varphi(1_{n})b\|_{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}<\|b\|\sqrt{\omega(b)+\varepsilon}.

(e 8.8)

Moreover, if
QT(A)=T(A),
then

\displaystyle\|b-\varphi(1_{n})b\|_{{}_{2,\overline{T(A)}^{w}}}<{{\min\{\|b\|,\|b\|_{{{}_{2,\overline{T(A)}^{w}}}}\}}}\sqrt{\omega(b)+\varepsilon}.

(e 8.9)

Proof.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
and
n\in\mathbb{N}.
Since
\Gamma:{\rm Cu}(A)\to{\rm LAff}_{+}(\overline{QT(A)}^{w})
is surjective,

\Gamma|_{{\rm Cu}(A)_{+}}
is also surjective (see (
2.13
)).
Note that
(1/n)\widehat{[b]}\in{\rm LAff}_{+}(\overline{QT(A)}^{w}).

We may choose
b_{1}\in(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}^{\bf 1}

such that
d_{\tau}(b_{1})=(1/n)d_{\tau}(b)
for all
\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}
and
b_{1}
is not Cuntz equivalent
to a projection.
We compute that
\omega(b_{1})=(1/n)\omega(b)
(see (
e 4.5
)).
Choose
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup\{d_{\tau}(b_{1})-\tau(f_{2\delta}(b_{1})):\tau\in{{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}\}<\omega(b_{1})+\varepsilon/4n.

(e 8.10)

Put
b_{2}=f_{\delta}(b_{1})
and

d_{1}={\rm diag}(\overbrace{b_{1},b_{1},...,b_{1}}^{n})\in A\otimes{\cal K}.

Then

\displaystyle f_{\delta}(d_{1})={\rm diag}(\overbrace{{{b_{2},b_{2},....,b_{2}}}}^{n})\in A\otimes{\cal K}.

(e 8.11)

Since
b_{1}
is not Cuntz equivalent to a projection,
for any
0<\eta<\delta/2,

d_{\tau}(f_{\eta}(d_{1}))<d_{\tau}(b)
for all
\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}.

Since
A
has strict comparison,
by

[
43
, Proposition 2.4 (iv)]
, there is
x\in A\otimes{\cal K}
such that

\displaystyle x^{*}x=f_{\delta}(d_{1})\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,xx^{*}\in{\rm Her}(b).

(e 8.12)

Then one obtains an isomorphism

{{\psi:{\rm Her}(x^{*}x)\to{\rm Her}(xx^{*})\subset{\rm Her}(b)\,\,\,{\rm{such\,\,that}}\,\,\,\psi(f(x^{*}x))=f(xx^{*})\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,f\in C_{0}((0,\|x\|^{2}]).}}

It induces a homomorphism
\varphi_{c}:C_{0}({\rm sp}(f_{\delta}({{b_{1}}})))\otimes M_{n}\to{\rm Her}(b)

such that
\varphi_{c}(\imath\otimes 1_{n})=xx^{*},
where
\imath\in C_{0}(f_{\delta}({{b_{1}}}))
is the identity function on
{\rm sp}({{f_{\delta}(b_{1})}}).

Define
a c.p.c. order zero map

\varphi:M_{n}\to\mathrm{Her}(b)
by
\varphi(e_{i,j})=\varphi_{c}(\imath\otimes{{e_{i,j}}})
(
1\leq i,j\leq 1
).

Let
p
be the open projection in
A^{**}
corresponding to
b
which may be identified
with the identity of
\widetilde{\mathrm{Her}(b)}.

We extend each
\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w}
to a 2-quasitrace on
\widetilde{\mathrm{Her}(b)}
(see II.2.5 of
[
2
]
)
such that
\tau(p)=\|\tau|_{\mathrm{Her}(b)}\|=d_{\tau}(b).

Then, we have, for all
\tau\in\overline{QT(A)}^{w},

\displaystyle\tau((p-\varphi(1_{n}))^{2})\leq\tau(p-\varphi(1_{n}))=\tau(p)-\tau(\varphi(1_{n}))

(e 8.13)

\displaystyle=d_{\tau}(b)-\tau(f_{\delta}(d_{1}))<\omega(b)+\varepsilon.

(e 8.14)

It follows that

\displaystyle\|b-\varphi(1_{n})b\|_{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}\leq\|b\|\|p-\varphi(1_{n})\|_{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}<\|b\|\sqrt{\omega(b)+\varepsilon}.

(e 8.15)

In the case that
QT(A)=T(A),
one
can also apply Cauchy–Bunyakovsky–Schwarz inequality (and (
e 8.14
)) to
obtain (
e 8.9
).
∎

Theorem 8.5
.

Let
A
be a
\sigma
-unital simple non-elementary
C^{*}
-algebra  with

QT(A)\setminus\{0\}\not=\emptyset.

Suppose that
A
has strict comparison
and T-tracial approximate oscillation zero.
Then
A
has property (TM).

Proof.

Choose
e\in{\rm Ped}(A)_{+}\setminus\{0\}
and define

A_{1}=\mathrm{Her}(e).
By Brown’s stable isomorphism theorem (
[
4
]
),
A_{1}\otimes{\cal K}\cong A\otimes{\cal K}.

To show that
A
has property (TM), it suffices to show that
A_{1}
has property (TM).
To simplify notation, without loss of generality, we may assume that
A={\rm Ped}(A).

Fix
a\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})
such that
0\leq a\leq 1.

Since
A
has T-tracial approximate oscillation zero,
\Omega^{T}(a)=0.
It follows that there is
a sequence
c_{k}\in{\rm Her}(a)
with
0\leq c_{k}\leq 1
such
that

\displaystyle\Pi(\iota(a))=\Pi(c)\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,\lim_{k\to\infty}\omega(c_{k})=0,

(e 8.16)

where
c=\{c_{k}\}
and
\Pi:l^{\infty}(A)\to l^{\infty}(A)/I_{{}_{\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}
is the quotient map.
By Theorem
7.11
,
\Gamma
is surjective. Then, applying
Lemma
8.4
, we obtain a c.p.c. order zero
map
\varphi_{k}:M_{n}\to{\rm Her}(c_{k})\subset{\rm Her}(a)

such that

\displaystyle\|c_{k}-\varphi_{k}(1_{n})c_{k}\|_{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}\leq\sqrt{\omega(c_{k})+1/k^{2}},\,\,k=1,2,....

(e 8.17)

Fix
1>\varepsilon>0.
Choose
k_{0}\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\omega(c_{k})+1/k^{2}}<{{(\varepsilon/3)^{3}}}.

(e 8.18)

Since
\Pi(\iota(a))=\Pi(c),
there exists
k_{1}\geq k_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\|a-c_{k}\|_{{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}}<(\varepsilon/3)^{3}\,\,\,{\rm for\,\,\,all}\,\,\,k\geq k_{1}.

(e 8.19)

Choose
b=c_{k_{1}+1}.
Then, for
k\geq k_{1},

\displaystyle\|a-\varphi_{k}(1_{n})a\|_{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}^{2/3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|a-b\|_{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}^{2/3}+\|b-\varphi_{k}(1_{n})b\|_{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}^{2/3}

\displaystyle+\|\varphi_{k}(1_{n})(b-a)\|_{{}_{2,\overline{QT(A)}^{w}}}^{2/3}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\varepsilon/3+\varepsilon/3+\varepsilon/3<\varepsilon.

∎

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
A
be a
\sigma
-unital algebraically simple
C^{*}
-algebra
with
QT(A)\not=\emptyset.

Suppose that
A
has property (TM) and
a\in{\rm Ped}(A\otimes{\cal K})_{+}^{\bf 1}\setminus\{0\}.

Then, for any integer
n,r\in\mathbb{N},
any
k\in\mathbb{N}
(
k\geq 2
) and
\varepsilon>0,
there exist an integer
m_{k}\geq r\in\mathbb{N}
and
mutually orthogonal elements
